|url=http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=eventdetail.home&eid=1782 |title=Upcoming Events: UFC 92 The Ultimate 2008 |publisher=Ufc.com |access-date=2008-12-19 |url-status=dead Sports |access-date=December 18, 2008}}\n\n==Background==\nThe main event featured [[UFC Light Heavyweight Championship|UFC Light Heavyweight Champion]] [[Forrest Griffin]], against undefeated challenger [[Rashad Evans]]. This was the first time that two winners of [[The Ultimate Fighter]] competed in a title fight against each other.{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=news.detail&gid=15300 |title=Three Historic Fights Announced for UFC 92: The Ultimate 2008 |publisher=UFC.com |date=October 21, 2008 |access-date=2008-12-19 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20081201062043/http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=news.detail&gid=15300 |archive-date=December 1, 2008 }}\n\nThe first co-main event saw former [[UFC Heavyweight Championship|UFC Heavyweight Champion]] [[Frank Mir]] against former [[Pride|Pride Heavyweight Champion]] and [[UFC Heavyweight Championship|Interim UFC Heavyweight Champion]] [[Antônio Rodrigo Nogueira]].{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/events/UFC-92-The-Ultimate-2008-8732|title=Fight Finder – UFC 92 The Ultimate 2008|date=December 27, 2008|publisher=Sherdog.com|access-date=December 27, 2008}}{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/articles/evans-mir-crowned-rampage-gets-revenge-15607 |title=Evans, Mir Crowned; Rampage Gets Revenge |last=Hall |first=Joe |publisher=Sherdog.com |access-date=December 28, 2008}}\n\nThe other co-main event was a rubber match between former [[UFC Light Heavyweight Champion]] [[Rampage Jackson|Quinton Jackson]] and former [[PRIDE Fighting Championships|PRIDE Middleweight champion]] [[Wanderlei Silva]].{{cite web|url=http://www.mmafrenzy.com/2008/09/02/ufc-media-conference-call-live-coverage/|last=Karkoski |first=Kris| date=September 2, 2008 |title=UFC 91 Media Conference Call |publisher=MMAfrenzy.com}} Silva had won their previous [[Wanderlei_Silva_vs._Quinton_Jackson|two matches]] in the [[Pride Fighting Championships|PRIDE]] organization in [[Japan]].\n\n[[Antoni Hardonk]] was originally scheduled to face Mark Burch, who was injured and replaced by [[Mike Wessel]].{{cite web |url=http://www.mmanews.com/ufc/Mike-Wessel-To-Replace-Mark-Burch-Against-Hardonk-At-UFC-92.html |title=Mike Wessel To Replace Mark Burch Against Hardonk At UFC 92 |work=MMAnews.com |access-date=December 28, 2008 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20081219160421/http://www.mmanews.com/ufc/Mike-Wessel-To-Replace-Mark-Burch-Against-Hardonk-At-UFC-92.html |archive-date=December 19, 2008 }}\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main card (PPV)}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Light Heavyweight\n|[[Rashad Evans]]\n|def.\n|[[Forrest Griffin]] (c)\n|KO (punches)\n|3\n|2:46\n|For the [[UFC Light Heavyweight Championship]]. [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] (UFC) on 17 January 2009, at [[The O2, Dublin|The O2, Dublin]] in [[Dublin, Ireland]].\n\n==Background==\nThe main event featured a [[Light heavyweight (MMA)|Light Heavyweight]] bout between former [[UFC Middleweight Championship|UFC Middleweight Champion]] [[Rich Franklin]] and former [[PrideFC|Pride Middleweight Champion]] and Pride Welterweight Champion [[Dan Henderson]]. The winner of the fight would become the coach opposite [[Michael Bisping]] for the ninth season of [[The Ultimate Fighter: United States vs. United Kingdom]].\n\nThe co-main event featured a [[Light heavyweight (MMA)|Light Heavyweight]] rematch between [[Maurício Rua]], the [[Pride|2005 Pride Middleweight Grand Prix Champion]], and the 2000 Pride Open-Weight Grand Prix Champion, [[Ultimate Fighting Championship#UFC Hall of Fame|UFC Hall of Famer]], and former [[UFC Heavyweight Championship|UFC Heavyweight Champion]] [[Mark Coleman]]. This fight was a rematch of their first controversial match at [[Pride 31]] which resulted in a brawl between the camps of both [[Chute Boxe Academy]] and [[Team Hammer House]].\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main card (PPV)}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Light Heavyweight\n|[[Dan Henderson]]\n|def.\n|[[Rich Franklin]]\n|Decision (split) (29–28, 27–30, 93"}]},{"pageid":16023995,"ns":0,"title":"UFC 94","revisions":[{"slots":{"main":{"contentmodel":"wikitext","contentformat":"text/x-wiki","content":"{{short description|UFC mixed martial arts event in 2009}}\n{{Infobox MMA event\n|name=UFC 94: St-Pierre vs. Penn 2\n|image= Ufc 94 poster.jpg\n|promotion=[[Ultimate Fighting Championship]]\n|date=January 31, 2009\n|venue=[[MGM Grand Garden Arena]]{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/event/146/ufc-94-superbowl-weekend.mma |title=UFC 9thoSt-Pierre vs. Penn II – Events |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2009-01-31 |access-date=2009-04-14 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090316081027/http://mmajunkie.com/event/146/ufc-94-superbowl-weekend.mma |archive-date=2009-03-16 }}\n|city=[[Las Vegas, Nevada]]\n|attendance=14,885\n|gate=$4,290,020\n|purse=$1,091,000\n|buyrate=920,000{{cite web|last=Meltzer |first=Dave |url=https://sports.yahoo.com/mma/news?slug=dm-ppvbiz021510&prov=yhoo&type=lgns |title=UFC remains king of PPV hill |publisher=sports.yahoo.com |date=2010-02-15 |access-date=2010-02-18}}\n|previousevent=[[UFC 93|UFC 93: Franklin vs. Henderson]]\n|followingevent=[[UFC Fight Night: Lauzon vs. Stephens]]\n}}\n\n'''''UFC 94: St-Pierre vs. Penn 2''''' was a [[mixed martial arts]] (MMA) [[pay-per-view]] event promoted by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] (UFC) on January 31, 2009. The card featured five televised MMA bouts, as well as five un-aired preliminary bouts. It was the second UFC event of 2009 and took place on the weekend of [[Super Bowl XLIII]].\n\nThe main event featured the [[UFC Welterweight Championship|UFC Welterweight Champion]], [[Georges St-Pierre]], defending his title against [[UFC Lightweight Championship|UFC Lightweight Champion]] and former UFC Welterweight Champion [[B.J. Penn]]. This represented the first time two current titleholders competed against each other in the UFC. The contest was heavily promoted, featuring a publicity tour to [[Canada]] and [[Hawaii]], as well as the introduction of ''[[UFC Primetime]]'', a preview show that cost $1.7 million to produce. The co-main event of the evening was a battle between two undefeated light heavyweights sporting 13–0 records in [[Lyoto Machida]] and [[Thiago Silva (fighter)|Thiago Silva]].\n\n==Background==\n\nThe main event featured the UFC Welterweight Champion Georges St-Pierre defending his championship against UFC Lightweight Champion B.J. Penn in a fight contested at the UFC welterweight limit of 170 pounds (77.1 kg). The fight represented the first time two current title holders faced off in the UFC.{{cite web|last=Meltzer |first=Dave |author-link=Dave Meltzer |url=https://sports.yahoo.com/mma/news?slug=dm-gsp012609&prov=yhoo&type=lgns |title=St-Pierre ready for his biggest challenge – MMA – Yahoo! Sports |publisher=Yahoo! Sports |date=2009-01-26 |access-date=2009-04-11}} Penn also sought to become the first fighter in the UFC to concurrently hold two championships in two separate weight classes. The ''UFC 94'' main event was met with a high level of anticipation from both the media and the fans. [[Sherdog]] called the fight between St-Pierre and Penn the most anticipated bout of 2009,{{cite web|author=Sherdog.com |url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/st-pierre-penn-official-for-ufc-94-16001 |title=St-Pierre, Penn Official for UFC 94 |publisher=Sherdog.com |access-date=2009-04-14}} and [[NBC Sports]] described it as one of the most anticipated fights in MMA history.{{cite web |url=http://nbcsports.msnbc.com/id/28895791/ |title=Inside the key matchups of UFC 94 – Mixed Martial Arts |publisher=nbcsports.msnbc.com |date=2009-01-28 |access-date=2009-04-14 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090402183230/http://nbcsports.msnbc.com/id/28895791 |archive-date=2009-04-02 |url-status=dead }} In an article previewing the fight, [[Dave Meltzer]] discussed the over-usage of exaggerations to promote contests in combat sports, but argued that the January 31 match between St-Pierre and Penn was \"one of the true epic matches in the history of the sport.\" The fans voiced their opinion in a poll conducted by ''[[Inside MMA]]'', where 45 percent of the viewers voted the match up between St-Pierre and Penn as the most anticipated fight to take place between December and February. The poll also featured (in descending order according to the results) [[Fedor Emelianenko]] vs. [[Andrei Arlovski]], [[Wanderlei Silva vs. Quinton Jackson]], and [[Rich Franklin]] vs. [[Dan Henderson]].{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/13406/inside-mma-poll-results-st-pierre-vs-penn-ii-is-mmas-most-anticipated-bout.mma |title=\"Inside MMA\" poll results: St-Pierre vs. Penn II is MMA's most anticipated bout |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2008-12-05 |access-date=2009-04-11 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090218125351/http://mmajunkie.com/news/13406/inside-mma-poll-results-st-pierre-vs-penn-ii-is-mmas-most-anticipated-bout.mma |archive-date=2009-02-18 }}\n\nSt-Pierre is a fighter with very strong wrestling, and trains with the Canadian Olympic team. His conditioning, strength and athleticism have also been identified as strong attributes.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=8091&zoneid=1 |title=UFC 94 in-depth: Georges St-Pierre vs. B.J. Penn 2|publisher=Mma Weekly |access-date=2009-04-14}} {{Dead link|date=October 2010|bot=H3llBot}}{{cite web|url=http://www.cbssports.com/mma/story/11315331/2 |title=Penn, St-Pierre vie for place in history – Mixed Martial Arts |publisher=CBSSports.com |access-date=2009-04-14}} He became the undisputed UFC Welterweight Champion by winning the interim-championship against [[Matt Hughes (fighter)|Matt Hughes]] at ''[[UFC 79]]'',{{cite web |last=Stupp |first=Dann |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/3421/ufc-79-georges-st-pierre-dominates-matt-hughes-for-interim-title-chuck-liddell-outlasts-wanderlei-silva.mma |title=UFC 79: Georges St-Pierre Dominates Matt Hughes for Interim Title; Chuck Liddell Outlasts Wanderlei Silva |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2007-12-30 |access-date=2009-04-14 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20080327183532/http://mmajunkie.com/news/3421/ufc-79-georges-st-pierre-dominates-matt-hughes-for-interim-title-chuck-liddell-outlasts-wanderlei-silva.mma |archive-date=2008-03-27 }} and successfully [[Championship unification|unifying]] the championship against Welterweight title holder [[Matt Serra]] at ''[[UFC 83]]'' in [[Quebec]],{{cite web|last=Meltzer |first=Dave |url=https://sports.yahoo.com/mma/news?prov=yhoo&slug=ys-ufclive041908&type=lgns |title=St-Pierre tops Serra at UFC 83 – MMA – Yahoo! Sports |publisher=Sports.yahoo.com |access-date=2009-04-14}} St-Pierre's home province. St-Pierre's fight prior to the ''UFC 94'' event was a successful title defense against [[Jon Fitch]] at ''[[UFC 87]]'' via unanimous decision, where he won every round on the judge's scorecards.{{cite web |url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=6834&zoneid=13 |title=UFC 87 results & photos |publisher=Mma Weekly |date=2008-08-09 |access-date=2009-04-14 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20080911202550/http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=6834&zoneid=13 |archive-date=2008-09-11 }} Penn is a fighter noted for his outstanding jiu-jitsu, as he is the first non-Brazilian to win the [[World Jiu-Jitsu Championship]] at the black belt level. His other notable strengths include boxing, and wrestling/takedown defense.{{cite web|url=https://ca.sports.yahoo.com/mma/news?slug=ki-010708mmabreakdown&prov=yhoo&type=lgns |title=Fight breakdown: B.J. Penn vs. Joe Stevenson – MMA – Yahoo! Canada Sports |publisher=Ca.sports.yahoo.com |access-date=2009-04-14}} Penn captured the vacant UFC Lightweight Championship by defeating [[Joe Stevenson]] at ''[[UFC 80]]''.{{cite web|author=Sherdog.com |url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/articles/stevenson-penned-by-the-prodigy-10879 |title=Stevenson Penned by the 'Prodigy' |publisher=Sherdog.com |access-date=2009-04-14}} The belt was vacant as a result of the [[California State Athletic Commission]] stripping then-champion [[Sean Sherk]] of the title for testing positive for anabolic steroid Nandrolone, or more commonly known as Decadurabolin, following a title defense.{{cite web|last=Meltzer |first=Dave |url=https://sports.yahoo.com/mma/blog/cagewriter/post/Sherk-stripped-of-lightweight-title?urn=mma,56811 |title=Sherk stripped of lightweight title – Cagewriter – Mixed Martial Arts – Yahoo! Sports |publisher=Sports.yahoo.com |date=2007-12-08 |access-date=2009-04-14}} Penn entered the ''UFC 94'' fight following a successful title defense as well, with a 3rd-round TKO of former champion Sean Sherk at ''[[UFC 84]]''.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=6326&zoneid=2 |title=UFC 84 results and live play-by-play |publisher=Mma Weekly |date=2008-05-24 |access-date=2009-04-14 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090131231543/http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=6326&zoneid=2 |archive-date=January 31, 2009 }} St-Pierre and Penn had fought once before in a welterweight number one contenders match at ''[[UFC 58]]'' on March 4, 2006, where St-Pierre won in a very evenly matched contest by split decision.\n[[Image:GeorgeStPierre.jpg|thumb|left|St-Pierre had defeated Penn in a previous bout; a split-decision at [[UFC 58]].]]\n\nPromotion for the St-Pierre and Penn contest began in November 2008, with UFC President [[Dana White]], and the two main event fighters embarking on publicity tours in [[Toronto]], [[Ontario]], Canada and [[Honolulu, Hawaii]]. The fighters answered various fan questions, and signed autographs.{{cite web|url=https://www.espn.com/extra/mma/news/story?id=3754319 |title=BJ penn rewriting history against Georges St-Pierre – ESPN |publisher=[[ESPN]] |date=2008-12-09 |access-date=2009-04-14}} In an effort to further promote the contest, the UFC introduced ''[[UFC Primetime]]'', a three-part series that cost $1.7 million to produce. ''[[UFC Primetime]]'' detailed the final preparations of St-Pierre and Penn's before their fight. [[Spike (TV channel)|Spike]] aired the half-hour episodes on the final three Wednesdays leading up to the fight, beginning on January 14, 2009 and concluding on January 28, 2009. Camera crews spent twenty five days with each fighter rather than the three days for a standard ''UFC Countdown'' preview show, and episodes aired shortly after they were filmed. The episodes were aired quickly to provide weekly updates on each fighter's current status.{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=news.detail&gid=17010 |title=Inside UFC Primetime – Series premieres on Spike TV this Wednesday |last=Gerbasi |first=Thomas |date=January 11, 2009 |work=UFC.com |access-date=January 13, 2009 }}{{Dead link|date=July 2018 |bot=InternetArchiveBot |fix-attempted=no }} After the first episode aired, it was reported by MMAjunkie.com that B.J. Penn was unhappy with his portrayal in the program, and wanted to withdraw from the project.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/13781/trouble-in-paradise-b-j-penn-cuts-short-ufc-primetime-taping.mma |title=Trouble in paradise? B.J. Penn cuts short \"UFC Primetime\" taping |date=January 21, 2009 |work=MMAJunkie.com |access-date=February 3, 2009 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090201160409/http://mmajunkie.com/news/13781/trouble-in-paradise-b-j-penn-cuts-short-ufc-primetime-taping.mma |archive-date=February 1, 2009 }} Penn made a subsequent appearance on TapouT radio to discuss this report and confirmed that he has taken issue with his portrayal on the show. He was adamant that he would not continue to allow filming unless his portrayal changed.{{cite web|url=http://www.matratz.com/2009/01/22/bj-penn-talks-with-tapout-radio/ |title=B.J. Penn talks with TapouT radio. |publisher=MatRatz.com |date=2009-01-22 |access-date=2009-04-11}} In the end, ''[[UFC Primetime]]'' proved to be a ratings success, as it was announced by Spike that the program was the most watched UFC preview show ever, drawing 1.4 million viewers for its first episode (880,000 viewers for first airing and 614,000 viewers for an immediate repeat).{{cite web |last=Stupp |first=Dann |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/13728/ufc-primetime-st-pierre-vs-penn-ii-highest-rated-countdown-show-ever.mma |title=\"UFC Primetime: St-Pierre vs. Penn II\" highest rated \"Countdown\" show ever |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2009-01-15 |access-date=2009-04-11 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090312060248/http://mmajunkie.com/news/13728/ufc-primetime-st-pierre-vs-penn-ii-highest-rated-countdown-show-ever.mma |archive-date=2009-03-12 }} The second episode averaged 825,000 viewers, while the third episode drew 662,000 viewers.{{cite web|author=Sherdog.com |url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/articles/4/The-Weekly-Wrap-Jan-24-Jan-30-16003 |title=The Weekly Wrap: Jan. 24 – Jan. 30 |publisher=Sherdog.com |access-date=2009-04-11}}\n\nThe co-main event featured a bout between two top contenders and undefeated light heavyweights, [[Lyoto Machida]] and [[Thiago Silva (fighter)|Thiago Silva]]. The two were originally scheduled to meet at ''[[UFC 89]]'', but a back injury forced Silva to withdraw from the contest.{{cite web|author=Sherdog.com |url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/injured-silva-withdraws-from-ufc-89-14296 |title=Injured Silva Withdraws from UFC 89 |publisher=Sherdog.com |access-date=2009-04-12}} UFC President [[Dana White]] indicated in the pre-fight press conference that Machida would receive a title shot with a victory, while Silva would need to defeat Machida and win one more contest before earning the same opportunity.{{cite web|last=Meltzer |first=Dave |url=https://sports.yahoo.com/mma/news?slug=dm-silvamachida013009&prov=yhoo&type=lgns |title=Machida-Silva a worthy UFC 94 understudy – MMA – Yahoo! Sports |publisher=Sports.yahoo.com |access-date=2009-04-12}} Machida is a fighter who prefers to counter-strike,{{cite web |url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=8070&zoneid=3 |title=UFC 94 in-depth: Lyoto Machida vs Thiago Silva |publisher=Mma Weekly |access-date=2009-04-12 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090403201330/http://mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=8070&zoneid=3 |archive-date=2009-04-03 }} and combines [[Shotokan Karate]], [[sumo wrestling]] and [[Brazilian jiu-jitsu]] to form his own style. Silva is a Brazilian jiu-jitsu black belt, but prefers to fight standing.{{cite web|url=http://www.setanta.com/uk/Articles/other-sports/2009/01/28/UFC-Machida-vs-Silva-preview/gnid-37514/ |title=UFC: Machida vs Silva preview |publisher=Setanta |access-date=2009-04-12 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090404173216/http://www.setanta.com/uk/Articles/other-sports/2009/01/28/UFC-Machida-vs-Silva-preview/gnid-37514/ |archive-date=April 4, 2009 }} Machida entered the ''UFC 94'' fight following a unanimous decision victory over former UFC Light Heavyweight Champion [[Tito Ortiz]] at ''UFC 84'', where he won every round on the judge's scorecards. Thiago Silva entered the contest with 1st-round TKOs over Antonio Mendes at ''UFC 84'', and [[Houston Alexander]] at ''[[UFC 78]]''.{{cite web |url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=5095&zoneid=13 |title=Rashad Evans wins at UFC 78 in a split |publisher=Mma Weekly |date=2007-11-18 |access-date=2009-04-14 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20081013004036/http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=5095&zoneid=13 |archive-date=2008-10-13 }}\n\nThe event also featured the return of two more fighters from injury, in [[Karo Parisyan]] and [[Stephan Bonnar]]. Parisyan, a [[judo]] standout,{{cite web |url=http://www.blackbeltmag.com/archives/69 |title=Black Belt Magazine |publisher=Blackbeltmag.com |date=1982-08-28 |access-date=2009-04-18 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20070810204605/http://www.blackbeltmag.com/archives/69 |archive-date=2007-08-10 }} had to withdraw from a scheduled fight against [[Yoshiyuki Yoshida]] at ''[[UFC 88]]'' with a back injury just one day before the fight.{{cite web|url=http://fiveouncesofpain.com/2008/09/05/ufc-88-bout-between-parisyan-vs-yohsida-scratched/ |title=UFC 88 bout between Parisyan vs. Yoshida scratched |publisher=Five Ounces of Pain |date=2008-09-05 |access-date=2009-04-18}} His opponent for ''UFC 94'' was undefeated South Korean welterweight [[Dong Hyun Kim]].{{cite web|url=http://fiveouncesofpain.com/2008/12/22/karo-parisyan-vs-dong-hyun-kim-now-official-for-ufc-94/ |title=Karo Parisyan vs. Dong Hyun Kim now official for UFC 94 |publisher=Five Ounces of Pain |date=2008-12-22 |access-date=2009-04-18}} Bonnar, a light heavyweight, was to take on [[Matt Hamill]] at [[UFC Fight Night 13]], but a left knee injury during training forced him out of that bout. His opponent, the 21-year-old [[Jon Jones]] was a national amateur wrestling champion and coming off a unanimous decision victory over Andre Gusmão at ''UFC 87'', where he took the fight on three weeks notice.{{cite web |last=Morgan |first=John |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/4943/less-than-four-months-into-mma-career-jon-jones-ready-for-ufc-87-debut.mma |title=Less than four months into MMA career, Jon Jones ready for UFC 87 debut |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2008-07-31 |access-date=2009-04-18 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090312060322/http://mmajunkie.com/news/4943/less-than-four-months-into-mma-career-jon-jones-ready-for-ufc-87-debut.mma |archive-date=2009-03-12 }} Rounding out the main card was a lightweight contest between the winner of the [[The Ultimate Fighter 5|fifth season]] of the UFC's reality television series and MMA competition ''[[The Ultimate Fighter]]'', [[Nate Diaz]], and contender [[Clay Guida]]. ''The Ultimate Fighter'' is a single-elimination reality series featuring fledgling professional MMA fighters competing for the title of The Ultimate Fighter, and a six-figure, multi-fight contract with the UFC. Diaz is a Brazilian jiu-jitsu [[Brazilian jiu jitsu#Grading|brown belt]] under Cesar Gracie, and Guida is noted by MMA journalist Sam Caplan as one of the UFC's most highly regarded fighters. The two were expected to fight in December 2008, but the fight failed to materialize due to a foot injury to Guida.{{cite web|url=http://fiveouncesofpain.com/2008/12/06/nate-diaz-expected-to-fight-clay-guida-at-ufc-94/ |title=Nate Diaz expected to fight Clay Guida at UFC 94 |publisher=Five Ounces of Pain |date=2008-12-06 |access-date=2009-04-18}}\n\n==Event==\n[[Image:LasVegas-MGMgrand.jpg|right|thumb|250px|14,885 fans filled the [[MGM Grand Garden Arena]] to capacity to witness ''UFC 94'' and the rematch between St-Pierre and Penn.]]\nThe card consisted of ten mixed martial arts bouts all sanctioned by the [[Nevada State Athletic Commission]] and contested under the [[Unified Rules of Mixed Martial Arts]]. All bouts consisted of three five-minute rounds except for the main-event championship match between St-Pierre and Penn which was five five-minute rounds. Five of the bouts were part of the non-televised preliminary card. The reported attendance was 14,885 with a total gate of $4,290,020.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/13880/ufc-94-scores-4-3-million-gate-with-14885-attendees.mma |title=UFC 94 scores $4.3 million gate with 14,885 attendees |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2009-02-01 |access-date=2009-04-16 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090227061419/http://mmajunkie.com/news/13880/ufc-94-scores-4-3-million-gate-with-14885-attendees.mma |archive-date=2009-02-27 }} The UFC announced on January 14, 2009 that the event was completely sold out, however MMAjunkie.com noted that at the time of announcement, it was still possible to find single tickets.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/13708/ufc-ufc-94-st-pierre-vs-penn-sold-out.mma |title=UFC announces \"UFC 94: St-Pierre vs. Penn\" is sold out |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2009-01-14 |access-date=2009-04-18 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090220151100/http://mmajunkie.com/news/13708/ufc-ufc-94-st-pierre-vs-penn-sold-out.mma |archive-date=2009-02-20 }} The reported buyrate of the pay-per-view was 800,000.{{cite web |last=Rome |first=Michael |url=http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2009/2/18/763136/sobering-buyrate-news-for |title=Sobering Buyrate News for UFC and Affliction |publisher=Bloody Elbow |date=2009-02-18 |access-date=2009-04-16 |archive-date=2009-02-21 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090221143433/http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2009/2/18/763136/sobering-buyrate-news-for |url-status=dead }}\n\n===Preliminary card===\nThe event's five preliminary fights all went the full three rounds, ending in [[Mixed martial arts#Victory|decisions]]. In the first bout of the evening, [[Dan Cramer]] became the first fighter to be successful in a professional mixed martial arts debut at a UFC pay-per-view event since [[Marcio Cruz]] at ''[[UFC 55]]''. In his fight with [[Matt Arroyo]],{{cite web|author=Sherdog.com |url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/articles/ufc-94-notebook-accusations-swarm-st-pierre-16019 |title=UFC 94 Notebook: Accusations Swarm St-Pierre |publisher=Sherdog.com |access-date=2009-04-16}} Cramer executed a takedown and attempted punches on the ground while avoiding the Brazilian Jiu-Jitsu brown-belt's submissions during the first two rounds, while the third and final round saw Arroyo taking Cramer's back on the ground and unsuccessfully going for a [[rear naked choke]]. Two of the three judges scored the bout 29–28 for Cramer while the remaining third judge saw it 28–29 for Arroyo, giving Cramer the split-decision victory.{{cite web |url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/ufc-94-gsp-vs-penn-ii-play-by-play-16002 |title=UFC 94 GSP vs. Penn II Play-by-Play |date=February 1, 2009 |work=Sherdog.com |access-date=February 1, 2009}}{{cite web|author=Sherdog.com |url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/articles/fitch-dominates-gono-tavares-ends-skid-at-ufc-94-16013 |title=Fitch Dominates Gono; Tavares Ends Skid at UFC 94 |publisher=Sherdog.com |access-date=2009-04-16}}\n\nIn the second bout of the event, [[Jake O'Brien (fighter)|Jake O'Brien]] rebounded from two TKO losses in a row with a split-decision win over [[Christian Wellisch]]. O'Brien, a former wrestler at Purdue University, was able to take Wellisch down and control his opponent on the ground but was hurt on the feet in the first and second rounds. The judges saw it 29–28, 28–29 and 29–28 in favor of O'Brien. This was both fighters first fight at light heavyweight after dropping down from heavyweight.\n\nIn one of the two bouts being awarded \"Fight of the Night\" bonuses, [[John Howard (fighter)|John Howard]] defeated [[Chris Wilson (fighter)|Chris Wilson]] by split decision in their welterweight contest. Howard was able to take down Wilson in all three rounds where he scored with ground-and-pound and once went for a rear naked choke. Wilson had some success on his feet, but Howard landed the harder strikes. The contest was scored twice 29–28 for Howard and once 28–29 for Wilson.{{cite web|url=http://mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=8094&zoneid=4 |title=Fitch dominates Gono; UFC 94 prelims go the distance |publisher=Mma Weekly |access-date=2009-04-16 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090409022509/http://mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=8094&zoneid=4 |archive-date=April 9, 2009 }}\n\nThe only lightweight bout on the preliminary card saw [[Thiago Tavares]] defeating [[Manny Gamburyan]] by unanimous decision. Tavares went for ground-and-pound after takedowns, while the smaller Gamburyan tried to push the pace on the feet but was unable to counter Tavares' more powerful strikes. All three judges scored it 29–28 in favor of Tavares.\n\nIn the last fight on the preliminary card, [[American Kickboxing Academy]]'s [[Jon Fitch]] faced [[Akihiro Gono]]. The bout saw Fitch continually taking down Gono and landing with ground-and-pound punches on the downed opponent. Fitch came close to submitting Gono in the first and second rounds when he went for a rear naked choke and an [[armbar]] respectively, but he was unable to finish the Japanese fighter. All three judges scored the bout in favor of Fitch, two scoring it 30–27 with one judge seeing it 30–26 giving Fitch the unanimous decision victory.\n\n===Main card===\n[[Image:UFC-Octagon-USMCPhoto.jpg|left|thumb|All fights took place inside a cage, the UFC's ''Octagon''.]]\nThe first bout on the televised portion of the event saw [[Nate Diaz]] taking on [[Clay Guida]] in a lightweight contest. Diaz, who had an eight-inch reach advantage on Guida, was able to frustrate him with punches on the feet while Guida has success with leg-kicks followed by a right hand straight.{{cite web|url=http://mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=8095&zoneid=3 |title=Jones impressive over Bonnar; Decisions reign supreme |publisher=MMAWeekly.com |date=2009-02-01 |access-date=2009-04-16 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090218230909/http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=8095&zoneid=3 |archive-date=February 18, 2009 }} After closing the distance, Guida used his wrestling experience to avoid submissions while controlling and pressuring Diaz both in the clinch and on the ground throughout the fight. Diaz rallied with punches in the final round but was unable to finish off Guida. The judges scored the bout 29–28 twice for Guida and once for Diaz for the split-decision victory.{{cite web|author=Sherdog.com |url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/articles/st-pierre-stops-penn-after-fourth-16014 |title=St-Pierre Stops Penn After Fourth |publisher=Sherdog.com |access-date=2009-04-16}} The bout was awarded the event's second \"Fight of the Night\" honor gaining both fighters a $65,000 bonus.\n\nThe second bout saw two judo [[Black belt (martial arts)|black belts]] fighting as [[Karo Parisyan]] went up against undefeated [[Dong Hyun Kim]] in a matchup at welterweight. Kim took Parisyan's back in the first round and threatened with submissions as Parisyan attempted to regain position, while the second round saw Parisyian executing a judo-throw and going for a [[Kimura lock|kimura]]-submission. The final round was mostly spent in the clinch with both fighters getting takedowns. Parisyian was awarded a split-decision victory with the scores 29–28, 28–29 and 29–28. However, the result was later changed to a \"no contest\" after Parisyian tested positive for three banned painkillers in the post-fight drug test also resulting in a nine months suspension and $32,000 fine.{{cite web|author=Sherdog.com |url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/Parisyan-Suspended-Nine-Months-16620 |title=Parisyan Suspended Nine Months |publisher=Sherdog.com |access-date=2009-04-16}}\n [[Image:StephanBonnar.png|right|thumb|Stephan Bonnar who returned to take on undefeated [[Jon Jones]] after just over a year absence.]]\nIn only his second fight for the UFC, [[Jon Jones]] took on [[The Ultimate Fighter 1]] runner-up [[Stephan Bonnar]] at light heavyweight in the third fight on the main card. Jones was able to execute multiple throws and takedowns during the first two rounds, at one point [[suplexing]] Bonnar. Jones also had the most success striking and was able to knock down his opponent with a spinning elbow-strike. Bonnar rallied with strikes against the tiring Jones in the third round, but the bout went the distance with the judges scoring it 29–28 once and 30–27 twice all in favor of Jones.\n\nThe fourth fight featured two undefeated Brazilians meeting at light heavyweight in [[Lyoto Machida]] and [[Thiago Silva (fighter)|Thiago Silva]]. Machida was able to knock down Silva twice during the first round before ultimately knocking him out after tripping him and jumping in landing the knockout punch at 4:59 of the first round. The finish marked the first win for Machida by knockout, and netted him a $65,000 bonus as it was awarded \"Knockout of the Night\".{{cite web |url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=8099 |title=UFC hands down $65,000 bonuses for UFC 94 |publisher=Mma Weekly |date=2009-02-01 |access-date=2009-04-16 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090205150156/http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=8099 |archive-date=2009-02-05 }}\n\nThe fifth and final bout of the main card was the UFC Welterweight Championship match between Georges St-Pierre and B.J. Penn, regulated by referee [[Herb Dean]]. The bout started out with the two fighters clinching and throwing knees. St-Pierre dropped down for a single-leg takedown but Penn defended while standing on one leg with his back to the cage. Unable to get the takedown, St-Pierre let go and connected with a right hand punch. They met up and clinched in the middle of the cage with St-Pierre pushing Penn towards the fence. There, St-Pierre attempted a takedown but went back the clinch when he was unable to finish it. The two fighters continued trading punches to end the first round.{{cite web |url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=8093&zoneid=2 |title=UFC 94 results, live play-by-play and photos |publisher=MMAWeekly.com |date=2009-01-31 |access-date=2009-04-16 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090409000946/http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=8093&zoneid=2 |archive-date=2009-04-09 }}\n\nRound two started with two fighters throwing strikes with St-Pierre landing a punch drawing blood from Penn's nose. St-Pierre then clinched up and tripped Penn to the ground. There Penn attempted to defend using the rubber guard, but St-Pierre passed to half-guard and eventually side-control while landing punches. The two continued [[grappling]] with Penn taking several ground-and-pound punches from St-Pierre until the round ended.\n\nThe third and fourth rounds saw St-Pierre continue his ground-and-pound on a visibly discouraged Penn, and at the end of the fourth, Penn's corner motioned for the cage-side doctor to stop the fight. The referee waved off the contest, officially declaring St-Pierre the winner, retaining his UFC Welterweight Championship, by TKO due to \"referee stoppage on doctor's advice\".{{cite web|url=http://boxing.nv.gov/2009%20Results%20Web/01-31-09%20MMA.pdf|date=January 31, 2009|access-date=February 3, 2009|title=Mixed martial arts show results, January 31, 2009|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090306171746/http://boxing.nv.gov/2009%20Results%20Web/01-31-09%20MMA.pdf|archive-date=March 6, 2009}}\n\n==St-Pierre greasing controversy==\n[[Image:St-PierreGrease.jpg|thumb|right|250px|[[Cornerman]] [[Phil Nurse]] applying Vaseline to St-Pierre's face, and illegally touching his fighter's back.]]\n\nThe contest between St-Pierre and Penn was a source of significant controversy. In between rounds one and two, as well as round three,{{cite web|url=http://fiveouncesofpain.com/2009/02/04/dana-white-addresses-vaseline-allegations-made-against-georges-st-pierre/ |title=Dana White addresses Vaseline allegations made against Georges St-Pierre |publisher=Five Ounces of Pain |date=2009-02-04 |access-date=2009-04-14}} one of St-Pierre's cornermen, [[Phil Nurse]], was seen applying [[petroleum jelly]] (brand name Vaseline) on the shoulder and the back of St-Pierre by members of the [[Nevada State Athletic Commission]] (NSAC). Vaseline is used to aid cuts in boxing as well as MMA, but applying it to parts of the body other than the face is illegal because of the element of grappling in the sport. A fighter that is \"slippery\" as a result of the vaseline will have an added advantage, because friction and grip are compromised.{{cite web|url=http://www.lasvegassun.com/news/2009/feb/03/grease-gate-clouds-st-pierres-dominant-win/ |title='Grease-Gate' clouds superfight |publisher=[[Las Vegas Sun]] |access-date=2009-04-14}} UFC President Dana White addressed the issue during a radio appearance in Boston, Massachusetts. He indicated that he did not believe the vaseline influenced the outcome of the fight, but still expected Nurse to be punished.\n\nPenn's brother revealed two days after the bout that the Penn camp would be making a formal complaint to the NSAC. In addition, Penn's head coach, Rudy Valentino alleged that St-Pierre also had an illegal substance on his body during his April 2008 rematch with Matt Serra.{{cite web |url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/articles/ufc-94-notebook-accusations-swarm-st-pierre-16019 |title=UFC 94 Notebook: Accusations Swarm St-Pierre |last=Knapp |first=Brian |date=February 2, 2009 |work=Sherdog.com |access-date=February 3, 2009}} NSAC Executive Director Keith Kizer confirmed that improprieties had occurred in St-Pierre's corner. He witnessed St-Pierre's cornerman [[Phil Nurse]] apply Vaseline to St-Pierre's face and then immediately rub St-Pierre's shoulders and back before Nurse wiped his hands. Kizer had St-Pierre's back wiped down after the second round, and confirmed after the third round that no Vaseline was being applied. Kizer was critical of the cornerman's action, but expressed doubts that the controversy would overturn St-Pierre's victory.{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/nsac-comments-on-vaseline-controversy-16028 |title=NSAC Comments on Vaseline Controversy |last=Knapp |first=Brian |date=February 2, 2009 |work=Sherdog.com |access-date=February 3, 2009}}\n\nOn March 12, 2009, B.J. Penn filed a formal request for investigation to the NSAC stating that St-Pierre used an illegal \"greasing\" agent on his body during the fight.{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/penn-files-request-for-nsac-investigation-16059|title=Penn Files Request for NSAC Investigation|last=Hunt|first=Loretta|date=February 3, 2009|work=Sherdog.com|access-date=2009-02-03}} The formal, written complaint consisted of twenty pages. It began by stating that \"other renowned fighters\" have also had similar experiences with St-Pierre, that his body was \"unnaturally slippery.\" It continued by accounting an apparent \"pre-bout warning\" made by Penn's camp to the NSAC that St-Pierre will engage in the illegal application of vaseline. Next, a request to monitor St-Pierre and members of the team to prevent future misconduct was made, namely a monitored shower of St-Pierre and/or hygiene cleansing. The written statement concluded with Penn seeking fines against trainer [[Jackson's Submission Fighting|Greg Jackson]], Nurse, and \"other unnamed individuals\" of $250,000 each. In addition, that St-Pierre's license be suspended or revoked, and that the result of the bout be changed to a no-contest.{{cite web |url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=8367&zoneid=4 |title=Did BJ Penn get fair shot? The complaint details |publisher=Mma Weekly |access-date=2009-04-14 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090415193441/http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=8367&zoneid=4 |archive-date=2009-04-15 }}\n\nSt-Pierre's trainer Greg Jackson denied any wrongdoing, stating that Nurse was rubbing and tapping on St-Pierre's body as part of a drill to aid in his breathing techniques. Jackson said that any Vaseline transferred from residue on Nurse's fingers was accidental and wiped off when pointed out.{{cite web |url=http://www.mmaontap.com/mma/entry/ufc-94-notes-penn-set-to-file-complaint-with-nsac-over-gsp-greasing/ |title=UFC 94 Notes: Penn Set to File Complaint With NSAC Over GSP Greasing |last=Chandler |first=John |date=February 2, 2009 |work=MMAontop.com |access-date=February 3, 2009}} Georges St-Pierre spoke to ''[[Sports Illustrated]]'' with regard to the controversy stating, \"I haven't seen the fight yet, but I remember at some point something happened and the athletic commission was complaining. They used a towel to wet my back and wipe it off. I don't mind. I didn't put Vaseline on myself and I'm not a cheater.\"{{cite web|url=http://sportsillustrated.cnn.com/2009/mma/02/02/georges.st.pierre.qa/index.html |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090205113335/http://sportsillustrated.cnn.com/2009/mma/02/02/georges.st.pierre.qa/index.html |url-status=dead |archive-date=February 5, 2009 |title=St-Pierre opens up about UFC 94 victory and controversy |last=Gross |first=Josh |date=February 2, 2009 |work=Sportsillustrated.cnn.com |access-date=February 3, 2009}} However, St-Pierre offered Penn a rematch in the summer that Penn verbally accepted but both had scheduled title defenses and the bout never came to fruition.{{cite web |url=http://blogs.chron.com/fighting/2009/02/gsp_is_ready_for_gsp_vs_penn_i.html |first=Jared |last=Barnes |date=February 4, 2009 |title=GSP is ready for GSP vs Penn III (w/ rash guards) |publisher=Hearst Corporation |work=Chron.com: Houston Chronicle website |access-date=2009-02-17 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090209174824/http://blogs.chron.com/fighting/2009/02/gsp_is_ready_for_gsp_vs_penn_i.html |archive-date=2009-02-09 |url-status=dead }}\n\nOn March 17, 2009, the NSAC held a formal hearing about the matter. Penn, his mother Lorraine Shin, and his attorney were present to make statements. Greg Jackson, Phil Nurse, and St-Pierre's attorney participated in the hearing as the opposing side.{{cite web|author=yacman |url=http://www.lasvegassun.com/news/2009/mar/17/grease-gate-still-slippery-after-hearing/ |title='Grease Gate' still a slippery subject |date=17 March 2009 |publisher=Las Vegas Sun |access-date=2009-04-14}} St-Pierre himself was not present.{{cite web|url=http://www.thefightnetwork.com/news/mma/2968/no_resolution_to_penngsp_greasegate_saga |title=The Fight Network |publisher=The Fight Network |date=2009-03-17 |access-date=2009-04-14}} Shin read from a written statement, urging the NSAC to \"do the right thing\", and punish St-Pierre to the \"fullest extent of authority.\"{{cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2009/03/penns-mother-urges-nsac-to-punish-st-pierre-team-to-fullest-extent-of-authority|title=Penn's mother urges NSAC to punish St. Pierre \"to fullest extent of authority\"|author=John Morgan|date=March 17, 2009|access-date=2020-02-16}} Jackson and Nurse apologized for the incident, but maintained that they did not knowingly cheat. At the hearing's conclusion, the NSAC Executive Director stated that St-Pierre's victory would not be changed to a no contest, nor would other sanctions, such as the loss of licenses be taken, unless a member of the NSAC files a disciplinary complaint against any of the accused. However, the committee left it ambiguous whether the matter was conclusively closed. Representatives from the Penn camp indicated that they were \"unsure\" of their next move.{{cite web|author=Tom Hamlin, MMAWeekly.com |url=http://sportsillustrated.cnn.com/2009/mma/03/18/penn.st.pierre.change/index.html |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090321104043/http://sportsillustrated.cnn.com/2009/mma/03/18/penn.st.pierre.change/index.html |url-status=dead |archive-date=March 21, 2009 |title=B.J. Penn, Georges St-Pierre greasing saga could spur change – SI.com – Mixed Martial Arts |publisher=Sportsillustrated.cnn.com |date=2009-03-18 |access-date=2009-04-14}}\n\n==Subsequent events==\n[[Image:Lyoto Machida.jpg|right|thumb|210px|Lyoto Machida took Jackson's place at ''UFC 98'' to challenge for the Light Heavyweight Championship.]]\nFollowing the main event at ''UFC 94'', top welterweight contender [[Thiago Alves (fighter)|Thiago Alves]] made his way into the cage to congratulate St-Pierre on the victory. After interviews with both fighters, it was announced that Alves would be next to challenge for St-Pierre's championship.{{cite web|last=Pugmire |first=Lance |url=https://www.latimes.com/archives/la-xpm-2009-feb-01-sp-ufc-mixed-martial-arts1-story.html |title=Georges St-Pierre dominates B.J. Penn |publisher=Articles.latimes.com |date=2009-02-01 |access-date=2009-04-14}} This fight took place as the co-main event of the ''[[UFC 100]]'' card.{{cite web|last=Herman |first=Gary |url=http://www.cbssports.com/mma/story/11330472 |title=UFC 94 notes: St-Pierre-Silva could happen in 2010 |publisher=CBSSports.com |date=2009-02-02 |access-date=2009-04-14}} St-Pierre's dominant victory also opened up discussion for a possible \"superfight\" with UFC Middleweight Champion [[Anderson Silva]] in 2010. Dana White stated that this bout was a possibility, and he would like to see it. St-Pierre expressed interest in the fight.\n\nAfter Penn's loss to St-Pierre, reports surfaced that the UFC Lightweight Champion was contemplating retirement.{{cite web|author=RSS feed |url=http://mma.fanhouse.com/2009/02/15/b-j-penn-focused-on-kenny-florian-hasnt-put-georges-st-pierr?icid=sphere_newsaol_inpage_fanhouse2 |title=B.J. Penn Focused on Kenny Florian, Hasn't Put Georges St-Pierre Behind Him |publisher=MMA FanHouse |date=2009-02-15 |access-date=2009-04-14}} However, during his first public appearance since the loss in Honolulu, Hawaii, he announced that he would continue to fight. He admitted that he was affected by the loss, and that \"a lot of things were going through his head.\" In regards to his next bout, Penn stated that he was in negotiation to fight top contender [[Kenny Florian]] in the summer of 2009.{{cite web|last=Pishna |first=Ken |url=https://ca.sports.yahoo.com/mma/news?slug=ys-mmaweek032809&prov=yhoo&type=lgns |title=Penn vs Florian set for UFC 101 |publisher=Ca.sports.yahoo.com |access-date=2009-04-14}} Penn would go on and defeat Florian at [[UFC 101]], winning in the fourth round by rear naked choke.\n\nIn the wake of the Vaseline controversy, the UFC responded by implementing immediate rule changes on February 7, 2009. These changes were discussed by longtime [[cutman]] Jacob \"Stitch\" Duran. According to Duran, under the new rules, only cutmen who are independent of both corners will be allowed to apply Vaseline to the fighter's faces. However, Duran was not able to confirm whether the rules will apply at all UFC events or just the events that take place in Nevada.{{cite web|url=http://fiveouncesofpain.com/2009/02/07/ufc-revises-rules-on-vaseline-application-by-cornermen/ |title=UFC revises rules on Vaseline application by cornermen |publisher=Five Ounces of Pain |date=2009-02-07 |access-date=2009-04-14}}\n\nDespite Lyoto Machida's knockout of Thiago Silva, Dana White indicated that he was not the number one contender for a title shot. Instead, a scheduled fight between former UFC Light Heavyweight Champion [[Rampage Jackson|Quinton Jackson]] and [[Keith Jardine]] would determine Machida's title fate. A victory for Jackson would earn him a fight with champion [[Rashad Evans]], but a win for Jardine would mean Machida will be awarded with a title shot.{{cite web |url=http://www.tatame.com/2009/02/16/To-get-titleshot-Machida-cheers-for-Jardine |title=To get titleshot, Machida cheers for Jardine |publisher=TATAME |date=2008-11-24 |access-date=2009-04-14 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090219173527/http://tatame.com/2009/02/16/To-get-titleshot-Machida-cheers-for-Jardine |archive-date=2009-02-19 }} Jackson won the fight via unanimous decision, but torn ligaments in his jaw forced the former champion out of the bout. Instead, Machida will challenge Evans for the UFC Light Heavyweight Championship at ''[[UFC 98]]''.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/14234/its-official-evans-to-meet-machida-not-jackson-at-ufc-98.mma |title=Done deal: Rashad Evans to meet Lyoto Machida, not Quinton Jackson, at UFC 98 |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2009-03-11 |access-date=2009-04-14 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://archive.today/20120525213331/http://mmajunkie.com/news/14234/its-official-evans-to-meet-machida-not-jackson-at-ufc-98.mma |archive-date=2012-05-25 }} Jackson will retain his title shot against the winner of that match upon returning from injury. Following the loss to Machida, Thiago Silva was scheduled to return to action against former champion [[Forrest Griffin]] at ''[[UFC 101]]'',{{cite web |last=Morgan |first=John |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/14279/forrest-griffin-returns-to-face-thiago-silva-at-ufc-101.mma |title=Forrest Griffin returns to face Thiago Silva at UFC 101 in August |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2009-03-16 |access-date=2009-04-14 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090320215250/http://mmajunkie.com/news/14279/forrest-griffin-returns-to-face-thiago-silva-at-ufc-101.mma |archive-date=2009-03-20 }} but his opponent was later changed to [[Keith Jardine]].{{cite web |last=Morgan |first=John |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/14718/keith-jardine-and-thiago-silva-agree-to-meet-at-ufc-102-in-august.mma |title=Keith Jardine and Thiago Silva agree to meet at UFC 102 in August |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2009-04-28 |access-date=2009-05-09 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090502150357/http://mmajunkie.com/news/14718/keith-jardine-and-thiago-silva-agree-to-meet-at-ufc-102-in-august.mma |archive-date=2009-05-02 }}\n\nKaro Parisyan's next fight was to be against American Kickboxing Academy's [[Josh Koscheck]] at ''UFC 98''. However, due to Parisyan's nine-month suspension from the NSAC for a failed drug test due to painkillers, the future of this fight is uncertain.{{cite web|url=http://fiveouncesofpain.com/2009/02/10/karo-parisyan-vs-josh-koscheck-possible-for-ufc-98/ |title=Karo Parisyan vs. Josh Koscheck possible for UFC 98 (UPDATED) |publisher=Five Ounces of Pain |date=2009-02-10 |access-date=2009-04-14}}\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main card (PPV)}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Welterweight\n|[[Georges St-Pierre]] (c)\n|def.\n|[[B.J. [[mixed martial arts]] event held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] (UFC) on February 21, 2009, at [[The O2 arena (London)|The O2 Arena]] in [[London]], [[United Kingdom]].\n\n==Background==\nThe event was broadcast live for viewers in [[Europe]] and [[Canada]] and later tape delayed for [[United States|U.S.]] viewers on [[Paramount Network|Spike]].{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=EventDetail.FightCard&eid=1877 |title=UFC 95 Sanchez vs. Stevenson |work=UFC.com |access-date=February 13, 2009 |url-status=dead winners of ''[[The Ultimate Fighter]]''.\n\n[[Neil Grove]] was originally scheduled to face [[Justin McCully]], but the bout was cancelled when McCully withdrew from the fight. Grove instead faced Mike Ciesnolevicz.{{cite web|url=http://www.tapology.com/fightcenter/events/ufc-95-sanchez-vs-stevenson |title=UFC 95 – Cancelled & Fizzled Bouts |publisher=tapology.com |access-date=2014-12-02}}\n\n[[Per Eklund (fighter)|Per Eklund]] was originally scheduled to face [[David Baron (fighter)|David Baron]], but the bout was cancelled when Baron withdrew from the fight. Eklund instead faced [[Evan Dunham]].\n\n[[Terry Etim]] was originally scheduled to face [[Justin Buchholz]], but the bout was cancelled when Buchholz had a [[Staphylococcal infection|staph infection]]. He was replaced by [[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC]] newcomer Brian Cobb.\n\n[[Stefan Struve]] made his UFC debut at this event.\n\nThe event drew an average of 2.4 million viewers on [[Spike (TV network)|Spike TV]].{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/14090/spike-tvs-ufc-95-broadcast-peaks-with-three-million-viewers.mma |title=Spike TV's UFC 95 broadcast peaks with three million viewers 95"}]},{"pageid":17817211,"ns":0,"title":"UFC 96","revisions":[{"slots":{"main":{"contentmodel":"wikitext","contentformat":"text/x-wiki","content":"{{short description|UFC mixed martial arts event in 2009}}\n{{Infobox MMA event\n|name=UFC 96: Jackson vs. Jardine\n|image=UFC96poster.jpg\n|promotion=[[Ultimate Fighting Championship]]\n|date=March 7, 2009\n|venue=[[Nationwide |title=UFC 96 Fighter Salaries: \"Rampage\" Tops Payroll |publisher=MMAFrenzy.com |date=2008-04-05 |access-date=2009-03-09}}\n|previousevent=[[UFC 95|UFC 95: Sanchez vs. Stevenson]]\n|followingevent=[[UFC Fight Night: Condit vs. Kampmann]]\n}}\n'''''UFC 96: Jackson vs. Jardine''''' was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] (UFC). It was held in [[Columbus, Ohio]], on March 7, 2009, at [[Nationwide Arena]].\n\n==Background==\nThe card was headlined by a Light Heavyweight bout between [[Rampage Jackson|Quinton Jackson]] and [[Keith Jardine]]. Following the main event, [[Rashad Evans]], training partner of Jardine and then-Light Heavyweight Champion, entered the cage and publicly challenged Jackson. It resulted in a long-standing feud between the two that would not be settled until 2010.{{cite web|url=http://www.tapology.com/fightcenter/events/ufc-96-jackson-vs-jardine |title=UFC 96 – Cancelled & Fizzled Bouts |publisher=tapology.com |access-date=2014-12-03}}\n\nA Welterweight bout between [[Dustin Hazelett]] and [[Ben Saunders (fighter)|Ben Saunders]] was originally scheduled for this card, but was cancelled when Hazelett was injured.\n\nSaunders was then scheduled to face replacement Ryan Madigan, but that bout was also cancelled when Saunders suffered a shattered toe in training. Madigan eventually faced [[Tamdan McCrory]] on this card.\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main card (PPV)}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Light Heavyweight\n|[[Rampage Jackson|Quinton Redemption\n|image=New97poster.jpg\n|promotion=[[Ultimate Fighting Championship]]\n|date=April 18, 2009\n|venue=[[Bell Centre]]\n|city=[[Montreal]], [[Quebec]], [[Canada]]\n|attendance= 21,451\n|gate= $4,900,000\n|buyrate=650,000{{cite web|last=Meltzer |first=Dave |url=https://sports.yahoo.com/mma/news?slug=dm-ppvbiz021510&prov=yhoo&type=lgns |title=UFC remains king of PPV hill |publisher=sports.yahoo.com |date=2010-02-15 |access-date=2010-02-18}}\n|previousevent=[[UFC Fight Night: Condit vs. Kampmann]]\n|followingevent=[[UFC 98|UFC 98: Evans vs. Machida]]\n}}\n\n'''''UFC 97: Redemption''''' (not to be confused with [[UFC 17|''UFC 17: Redemption'']]) was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] (UFC) on April 18, 2009, in [[Montreal]], [[Quebec]], [[Canada]].\n\n==Background==\nThis event broke the UFC attendance record that was set 364 days earlier in Montreal at ''[[UFC 83]]''.{{cite web|url=http://www.tapology.com/fightcenter/events/ufc-97-redemption |title=UFC 97: Redemption |publisher=tapology.com |access-date=2014-12-03}}\n\nDue to time constraints, the preliminary bout between [[Sam Stout]] and [[Matt Wiman]] was moved to the main card, resulting in a six-fight live main card.\n\n==Sanctioning and rules controversy==\nOn February 12, 2009, reports surfaced that province regulators (\"{{Lang|fr|[[Régie des alcools, des courses et des jeux]]|italic=no}}\", or RACJ) requested a set of rule changes that differ from the [[Mixed martial arts rules#Unified rules|Unified Rules of MMA]] put the event at risk from taking place.{{cite web |url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=8173&zoneid=2 |title=Possible Rule Changes Put UFC 97 in Jeopardy |date=February 12, 2009 |work=MMAWeekly.com |first=Ken |last=Pishna |access-date=February 12, 2009 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090216110144/http://mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=8173&zoneid=2 |archive-date=February 16, 2009 |url-status=dead |df=mdy-all }} The proposed rule changes banned elbow and knee strikes, mandated a smaller cage, and would stop a fight when a fighter gets knocked down.{{cite web|url=http://www.fighttrend.com/2009/02/13/possible-quebec-athletic-commission-rule-changes-puts-ufc-97-in-jeopardy/ |title=Possible Quebec Athletic Commission rule changes puts UFC 97 in jeopardy |date=February 12, 2009 |work=FightTrend.com |access-date=February 13, 2009}} {{Dead link|date=October 2010|bot=H3llBot}} Before, the RACJ has followed the Unified Rules, but as laws differ by city, state, or province, a commission can insist on the usage of its own rules.{{cite web |url=http://www.sportsnet.ca/mma/2009/02/12/ufc_qac/ |title=UFC's Montreal in jeopardy? |work=Sportsnet.ca |access-date=February 12, 2009 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090215182645/http://www.sportsnet.ca/mma/2009/02/12/ufc_qac/ |archive-date=February 15, 2009 |url-status=dead |df=mdy-all }}\n\nOn February 18, 2009, it was announced that UFC officials were able to convince the RACJ to reverse its stand and use the Unified Rules. As a concession, the UFC agreed to the banning of foot stomps for this event.{{cite web |last=Holland |first=Jesse |url=https://mmamania.com/2009/02/18/ufc-97-to-remain-in-montreal-after-compromise-with-quebec-athletic-commission/|title=UFC 97 to remain in Montreal on April 18 |date=February 18, 2009 |work=MMAmania.com |access-date=February 18, 2009}}\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main card (PPV)}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Middleweight\n|[[Anderson Silva]] (c)\n|def.\n|[[Thales held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] (UFC) on May 23, 2009, in [[Las Vegas Valley|Las Vegas]], [[Nevada]].{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=eventDetail.FightCard&eid=2001 |title=UFC 98 EVANS vs MACHIDA |publisher=Ufc.com |access-date=April 14, 2009 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090508064433/http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=eventDetail.FightCard&eid=2001 |archive-date=May 8, 2009 }}\n\n==Background==\nA title unification match between the Interim [[UFC Heavyweight Championship|UFC Heavyweight Champion]] [[Frank Mir]] and [[UFC Heavyweight Championship|UFC Heavyweight Champion]] [[Brock Lesnar]] was originally slated to be the main event, but was postponed until [[UFC 100]] due to a knee injury to Mir.{{cite web|first=Sam |last=Caplan |url=http://fiveouncesofpain.com/2009/03/06/injury-to-frank-mir-forces-cancellation-of-ufc-98-rematch-vs-brock-lesnar/ |title=Injury to Frank Mir forces cancellation of UFC 98 rematch vs. Brock Lesnar |publisher=Five Ounces of Pain |date=February 22, 1999 |access-date=March 7, 2009}}{{cite web |url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=8331&zoneid=13 |title=Frank Mir says May 23 date with Lesnar no go |publisher=Mma Weekly |date=March 7, 2009 |first=Ken |last=Pishna |access-date=March 7, 2009 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090310055904/http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=8331&zoneid=13 |archive-date=March 10, 2009 }}\n\nBy way of replacement, [[Rampage Jackson|Quinton Jackson]], former UFC Light Heavyweight champion, was to fight the undefeated current champion [[Rashad Evans]] but had to undergo [[arthroscopic surgery]] to repair ligament damage in his jaw that he received in a previous training camp. Instead, Evans headlined the card against fellow undefeated contender [[Lyoto Machida]].{{cite web |url=http://www.sportsnet.ca/mma/2009/03/09/jackson_evans/ |title=Jackson hurt, Evans fight still in doubt |publisher=Sportsnet.ca |access-date=March 10, 2009 |archive-date=March 14, 2009 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090314065846/http://www.sportsnet.ca/mma/2009/03/09/jackson_evans/ |url-status=dead }}{{cite news|author=Josh Gross, SI.com |url=http://sportsillustrated.cnn.com/2009/mma/03/11/evans.machida/index.html |archive-url=https://archive.today/20120714112604/http://sportsillustrated.cnn.com/2009/mma/03/11/evans.machida/index.html |url-status=dead |archive-date=July 14, 2012 |title=Rashad Evans to defend title against undefeated Lyoto Machida at UFC 98 – SI.com – Mixed Martial Arts |publisher=Sportsillustrated.cnn.com |date= March 11, 2009|access-date=March 11, 2009}}\n\nThe card featured the long anticipated grudge match between ''[[The Ultimate Fighter 6]]'' coaches [[Matt_Hughes_(fighter)|Matt Hughes]] and [[Matt Serra]].\n\nDue to injury, [[Josh Koscheck]] was not able to participate on this card. [[Brock Larson]] stepped in as the new opponent for [[Chris Wilson (fighter)|Chris Wilson]].{{cite web|url=http://www.411mania.com/MMA/news/101030/Josh-Koscheck-Injured,-Out-of-UFC-98-Bout-With-Chris-Wilson.htm |title=MMA – Josh Koscheck Injured, Out of UFC 98 Bout With Chris Wilson |publisher=411mania.com |access-date=April 3, 2009}}{{cite web|url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=8531&zoneid=13 |title=Brock Larson In For Koscheck at UFC 98 |publisher=MMA Weekly |access-date=April 7, 2009}} Chris Wilson was unable to complete the necessary medical paperwork in order to compete at the event and was replaced by [[Mike Pyle (fighter)|Mike Pyle]].{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/pyle-replaces-wilson-at-ufc-98-17585 |title=Pyle Replaces Wilson at UFC 98 |publisher=Sherdog |access-date=May 22, 2009}}\n\n[[James Irvin (fighter)|James Irvin]] was set to make his Middleweight debut against [[Drew McFedries]], but was not able to fight due to a knee injury.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=8683&zoneid=13 |title=James Irvin Off UFC 98 With Knee Injury |publisher=Mma Weekly |access-date=April 30, 2009}} Xavier Foupa-Pokam stepped in to fight McFedries.{{cite web |url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=8684&zoneid=13 |title=Xavier Foupa-Pokam Steps In To Fight McFedries |publisher=Mma Weekly |access-date=April 30, 2009 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090503142115/http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=8684&zoneid=13 |archive-date=May 3, 2009 }}\n\n[[Yushin Okami]] was set to fight [[Dan Miller (fighter)|Dan Miller]] on the main card but was taken off the card after Okami suffered a torn ligament. Early reports suggested [[Ed Herman (mixed martial artist)|Ed Herman]] would replace Okami,{{cite web|url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=8686&zoneid=13 |title=Ed Herman Replaces Yushin Okami at UFC 98 |publisher=Mma Weekly |access-date=May 1, 2009}} however [[Chael Sonnen]] stepped in to take the bout.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=8688&zoneid=13 |title=Herman Out, Sonnen In vs. Dan Miller at UFC 98 |publisher=MMA Weekly |access-date=May 1, 2009}}\n\n[[Houston Alexander]] was scheduled to fight [[André Gusmão]], but was forced to pull out due to a broken hand. [[Krzysztof Soszynski]] stepped in to fight Gusmao.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=8708&zoneid=13 |title=Soszynski Steps In for Alexander at UFC 98 |publisher=Mma Weekly |access-date=2009-05-05}}\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main card (PPV)}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Light Heavyweight\n|[[Lyoto Machida]]\n|def.\n|[[Rashad Evans]] 2009|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090923155358/http://mmajunkie.com/news/16268/ufc-fight-night-19-officially-draws-9490-attendees-for-577997-live-gate.mma|archive-date=September 23, 2009}}\n| attendance = 9,490\n| previousevent = [[UFC 102|UFC 102: Couture vs. Nogueira]]\n| followingevent = [[UFC 103|UFC 103: Franklin vs. Belfort]]\n}}\n'''''UFC Fight Night: Diaz vs. Guillard''''' (also known as '''''UFC Fight Night 19''''') was a [[mixed martial arts]] [[sporting event|event]] held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] (UFC) on September 16, 2009 in [[Oklahoma City]], [[Oklahoma]].{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=eventDetail.FightCard&eid=2272 |title=UFC Fight Night DIAZ vs GUILLARD |publisher=Ufc.com |access-date=2009-07-08 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090708235136/http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=EventDetail.FightCard&eid=2272 |archive-date=2009-07-08 }}\n\n==Background==\nA previously announced bout between [[Josh Neer]] and [[Kurt Pellegrino]] was moved to [[UFC 101]].{{cite web|url=https://mmamania.com/2009/03/22/josh-neer-vs-kurt-pellegrino-likely-moved-to-ufc-101-on-august-8/ |title=Josh Neer vs Kurt Pellegrino likely moved to UFC 101 on August 8 |date=22 March 2009 |publisher=Mmamania.com |access-date=2009-03-22}}\n\n[[Justin Buchholz]] replaced an injured Ronys Torres in a bout against [[Jeremy Stephens]].{{cite web|url=https://mmamania.com/2009/07/16/justin-buchholz-tapped-to-replace-an-injured-ronny-torres-against-jeremy-stephens-at-ufc-fight-night-19/ |title=Justin Buchholz tapped to replace an injured Ronny Torres against Jeremy Stephens at UFC Fight Night 19 |date=16 July 2009 |publisher=Mmamania.com |access-date=2009-07-16}}\n\n[[Ed Herman (mixed martial artist)|Ed Herman]] was supposed to replace an injured [[James Irvin (fighter)|James Irvin]] against [[Wilson Gouveia]] at [[UFC 102]] but then Gouveia suffered an injury. Herman instead faced [[Aaron Simpson (fighter)|Aaron Simpson]] at the event.{{cite web |url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=9291&zoneid=13 |title=HERMAN LIKELY TO STEP IN FOR IRVIN AT UFC 102 |publisher=Mma Weekly |access-date=2009-08-05 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090816083702/http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=9291&zoneid=13 |archive-date=2009-08-16 |url-status=dead }} [[Dan Miller (fighter)|Dan Miller]] was set to replace Herman. However, an injured Miller was replaced by [[Jay Silva]] against [[CB Dollaway]].{{cite web |url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=9525&zoneid=13 |title=NEWCOMER JAY SILVA STEPS IN TO FACE DOLLAWAY |publisher=Mma Weekly |access-date=2009-09-08 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090912074847/http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=9525&zoneid=13 |archive-date=2009-09-12 |url-status=dead }}\n\nAn injury forced [[Chris Lytle]] off the card.{{cite web|url=https://mmamania.com/2009/08/11/knee-injury-forces-chris-lytle-from-ufc-fight-night-19-bout-against-carlos-condit/ |title=Knee injury forces Chris Lytle to withdraw from UFC Fight Night 19 bout against Carlos Condit |date=11 August 2009 |publisher=Mmamania.com |access-date=2009-08-12}} [[Jake Ellenberger]] replaced him.{{cite web |url=http://mmafrenzy.com/11253/jake-ellenberger-will-fight-carlos-condit-at-ufc-fight-night-19/ |title=Jake Ellenberger Will Fight Carlos Condit at UFC Fight Night 19 |publisher=MMAFrenzy.com |date=2009-01-02 |access-date=2009-08-12 |archive-date=2018-07-09 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180709064648/https://www.mmafrenzy.com/11253/jake-ellenberger-will-fight-carlos-condit-at-ufc-fight-night-19/ |url-status=dead }}\n\nKyle Bradley pulled out of his matchup with [[Sam Stout]] due to injury{{cite web|url=https://mmamania.com/2009/08/18/kyle-bradley-injured-and-ruled-out-against-sam-stout-at-ufc-fight-night-19/ |title=Kyle Bradley injured and ruled out against Sam Stout at UFC Fight Night 19 |date=18 August 2009 |publisher=Mmamania.com |access-date=2009-08-19}} and was replaced with [[Phillipe Nover]]. However, on the night of the fight, Nover suffered from a seizure in the locker room. His match against Sam Stout was cancelled as a result.{{Cite web |url=http://www.411mania.com/MMA/news/116439/Phillipe-Nover-vs.-Sam-Stout-Off-Of-UFC-Fight-Night-19.htm |title=Phillipe Nover vs. Sam Stout Off Of UFC Fight Night 19 |publisher=411mania.com |first=Larry |last=Csonka |date=September 16, 2009 |access-date=September 16, 2009 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090924023256/http://www.411mania.com/MMA/news/116439/Phillipe-Nover-vs.-Sam-Stout-Off-Of-UFC-Fight-Night-19.htm |archive-date=September 24, 2009 }}\n\nThis event served as a lead-in to the premiere of ''[[The Ultimate Fighter: Heavyweights]]'' on [[Paramount Network|Spike]].\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main card}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Lightweight\n|[[Nate Diaz]]\n|def.\n|[[Melvin Guillard]]\n|Submission (guillotine choke)\n|2\n|2:13\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Lightweight\n|[[Gray Maynard]]\n|def.\n|[[Roger Huerta]]\n|Decision (split) (28–29, 30–27, 30–27)\n|3\n|5:00\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Welterweight\n|[[Carlos Condit]]\n|def.\n|[[Jake Ellenberger]]\n|Decision (split) (29–28, 28–29, 29–28)\n|3\n|5:00\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Middleweight\n|[[Nate Quarry]]\n|def.\n|[[Tim Credeur]]\n|Decision (unanimous) (29–28, 29–27, 29–28)\n|3\n|5:00\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent card|Preliminary card|header=no}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Light Heavyweight\n|[[Brian Stann]]\n|def.\n|[[Steve Cantwell]]\n|Decision (unanimous) (30–27, 30–27, 29–28)\n|3\n|5:00\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Welterweight\n|[[Mike Pyle (fighter)|Mike Pyle]]\n|def.\n|[[Chris Wilson (fighter)|Chris Wilson]]\n|Submission (guillotine choke)\n|3\n|2:15\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Middleweight\n|[[C. a [[mixed martial arts]] event held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] (UFC) on June 13, 2009, in [[Cologne]], [[Germany]].{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=eventDetail.FightCard&eid=2008 |title=UFC 99 The Comeback |publisher=Ufc.com |access-date=April 12, 2009}}\n\n==Background==\nThis was the first UFC event held in Germany,{{cite news|url=https://www.telegraph.co.uk/sport/othersports/boxing/5532048/UFC-99-Rich-Franklin-scores-unanimous-victory-over-Wanderlei-Silva-in-brutal-contest.html|title=UFC 99: Rich Franklin scores unanimous victory over Wanderlei Silva in brutal contest|date=2009-06-14|work=The Telegraph|access-date=2009-06-15 | location=London | first=Gareth A | last=Davies}} as well as the first in mainland Europe (events have previously been held in the United Kingdom, Ireland, Canada, the continental United States, Brazil, and Japan).\n\nOn Monday, June 1, the UFC announced that [[Mirko Cro Cop]] would be returning to the promotion to face [[Mostapha al-Turk]] at ''UFC 99''.{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=news.detail&gid=20176 |title=Cro Cop Returns against al-Turk on UFC 99 Card in Germany |publisher=UFC.com |access-date=June 1, 2009 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090603062736/http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=news.detail&gid=20176 |archive-date=June 3, 2009 }} It was Cro Cop's first fight in the UFC since his unanimous-decision loss to [[Cheick Kongo]] at ''[[UFC 75]]'' in 2007.\n\nAn announced bout between [[Heath Herring]] and [[Cain Velasquez]] was cancelled due to an illness that Herring was suffering. On May 20, 2009, [[Cheick Kongo]] was announced as the replacement.{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=news.detail&gid=19838 |title=Kongo Steps in for Herring, Faces Velasquez at UFC 99 |publisher=Ufc.com |access-date=May 20, 2009 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090523211915/http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=news.detail&gid=19838 |archive-date=May 23, 2009 }}\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main card (PPV)}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Catchweight (195 lb)\n|[[Rich Franklin]]\n|def.\n|[[Wanderlei Silva]]\n|Decision (unanimous) (30–27, 29–28, sports events in Germany]]"}}}],"langlinks":[{"lang":"ja","title":"UFC 99"}]},{"pageid":21101073,"ns":0,"title":"UFC 100","revisions":[{"slots":{"main":{"contentmodel":"wikitext","contentformat":"text/x-wiki","content":"{{short description|UFC mixed martial arts event in 2009}}\n{{pp-vandalism|small=yes}}\n{{Infobox MMA event\n|name=UFC 100\n|image=UFC100lesnarmir.jpg\n|promotion=[[Ultimate Fighting Championship]]\n|date=July 11, 2009\n|venue=[[Mandalay Bay Events Center]]\n|city=[[Las Vegas, Nevada|Las Vegas]], Nevada\n|attendance=10,871 (9,764 paid){{cite web |date=July 21, 2009 |url=http://boxing.nv.gov/results/Top_MMA_Gates/|title=Top MMA Gates |publisher=Nevada State Athletic Commission}}\n|purse=$1,790,000\n|gate=$5,101,740\n|buyrate=1,600,000{{cite news |title=The 51 best-selling pay-per-view fight nights in history |url=http://businessinsider.com/the-50-best-selling-pay-per-view-events-boxing-ufc-wrestling-tv-history-2017-8 |work=[[Business Insider]] |date=October 26, 2017}}\n|previousevent=[[The Ultimate Fighter: United States vs. United Kingdom Finale]]\n|followingevent=[[UFC 101|UFC 101: Declaration]]\n}}\n'''''UFC 100''''' was a [[mixed martial arts]] event produced by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] (UFC) on July 11, 2009, in [[Las Vegas Valley|Las Vegas]], [[Nevada]].\n\nThis event was the most bought UFC pay-per-view of all time with a buyrate of 1.6{{nbsp}}million,{{cite news |title=The 51 best-selling pay-per-view fight nights in history |url=http://businessinsider.com/the-50-best-selling-pay-per-view-events-boxing-ufc-wrestling-tv-history-2017-8 |work=[[Business Insider]] |date=October 26, 2017}} until [[UFC 202]] broke the record with 1.65{{nbsp}}million.\n\n==Background==\nThe event included two championship bouts and a fight between the two coaches on the [[Paramount Network|Spike TV]] show, ''[[The Ultimate Fighter]]''.\n\nThe main event was a rematch between UFC Heavyweight Champion [[Brock Lesnar]] and Interim UFC Heavyweight Champion [[Frank Mir]]. This [[championship unification]] bout occurred as a result of former UFC Heavyweight Champion [[Randy Couture]]'s year-long resignation from the promotion. During his absence, the UFC created an [[Interim championship]], which [[Antônio Rodrigo Nogueira]] won by defeating [[Tim Sylvia]]. Nogueira and Mir were opposing coaches on ''[[The Ultimate Fighter: Team Nogueira vs Team Mir]]'', and fought for the interim championship at ''[[UFC 92]]''. During the airing of the television program, Couture returned to the company and UFC President [[Dana White]] announced he would defend the title against Lesnar at ''[[UFC 91]]''. The winners of the Couture vs Lesnar and Nogueira vs Mir matches would fight for the undisputed title.{{cite web |last=Iole |first=Kevin |url=https://ca.sports.yahoo.com/mma/news?slug=ki-couture090208&prov=yhoo&type=lgns |title=Couture returns to UFC, faces Lesnar – MMA – Yahoo! Canada Sports |publisher=Ca.sports.yahoo.com |access-date=2009-05-15}} In his fourth professional fight, Lesnar defeated Couture via [[technical knockout]] to become the UFC Heavyweight Champion.{{cite web |last=Iole |first=Kevin |url=https://sports.yahoo.com/mma/news?slug=ki-mainevent111508&prov=yhoo&type=lgns |title=Lesnar drops Couture for UFC heavyweight crown – MMA – Yahoo! Sports |publisher=Sports.yahoo.com |access-date=2009-05-15}} Mir was crowned interim champion after becoming the first fighter to finish Nogueira.{{cite web |last=Sievert |first=Steve |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/13633/sizing-up-brock-lesnar-vs-frank-mir-ii.mma |title=Sizing up Brock Lesnar vs. Frank Mir II |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2009-01-05 |access-date=2009-05-15 |archive-date=2009-02-27 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090227045203/http://mmajunkie.com/news/13633/sizing-up-brock-lesnar-vs-frank-mir-ii.mma |url-status=dead }} The match between Lesnar and Mir was set for ''[[UFC 98]]''. However, Mir injured his knee while training, and withdrew from the bout.{{cite web |date=March 6, 2009 |url=http://fiveouncesofpain.com/2009/03/06/injury-to-frank-mir-forces-cancellation-of-ufc-98-rematch-vs-brock-lesnar/ |title=Injury to Frank Mir forces cancellation of UFC 98 rematch vs. Brock Lesnar |publisher=Five Ounces of Pain |access-date=2009-05-15}} The next day, it was announced the fight would be moved to ''UFC 100''.{{cite web |last=Meltzer |first=Dave |url=https://ca.sports.yahoo.com/mma/news?slug=dm-mainchange030909&prov=yhoo&type=lgns |title=UFC moves Lesnar-Mir to UFC 100 – MMA – Yahoo! Canada Sports |publisher=Ca.sports.yahoo.com |access-date=2009-05-15}} Lesnar and Mir had fought once before, at ''[[UFC 81]]'' (Lesnar's UFC debut); Mir won via [[kneebar]] submission.{{cite web |url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/UFC-81-Play-by-Play-11128 |title=UFC 81 Play-by-Play |publisher=Sherdog.com |access-date=2009-05-15}}\n\nThe co-main event featured [[Georges St-Pierre]], defending the [[UFC Welterweight Championship|Welterweight Championship]] against [[Thiago Alves (fighter)|Thiago Alves]]. Alves entered the fight after consecutive victories over three top UFC welterweights: [[Josh Koscheck]], [[Matt Hughes (fighter)|Matt Hughes]] and [[Karo Parisyan]].{{cite web |url=http://www.sherdog.com/fighter/Thiago-Alves-5998 |title=Fight Finder – Thiago \"Pitbull\" Alves's Mixed Martial Arts Statistics |publisher=Sherdog.com |access-date=2009-05-15}} St-Pierre had previously defended the title against [[Jon Fitch]] and [[UFC Lightweight Championship|UFC Lightweight Champion]] [[B.J. Penn]].\n\nThe third match on the main card was between top [[Middleweight (MMA)|middleweight]] contenders [[Dan Henderson]] and [[Michael Bisping]]. The two were opposing coaches on the [[The Ultimate Fighter: United States vs. United Kingdom|ninth season of ''The Ultimate Fighter'']] (Henderson on Team USA and Bisping on Team UK). Bisping had a professional MMA record of 17–1, and was undefeated since moving to middleweight. He entered the bout following wins over [[Charles McCarthy (fighter)|Charles McCarthy]], [[Jason Day (fighter)|Jason Day]] and [[Chris Leben]]. On June 2, it was announced the winner of the fight would get a shot at UFC Middleweight Champion [[Anderson Silva]].{{cite web |last=Stupp |first=Dann |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/15063/winner-of-ufc-100s-michael-bisping-vs-dan-henderson-fights-gets-a-title-shot.mma |title=Winner of UFC 100's Michael Bisping vs. Dan Henderson fight gets a title shot |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2009-06-02 |access-date=2009-07-13 |archive-date=2009-06-11 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090611150207/http://mmajunkie.com/news/15063/winner-of-ufc-100s-michael-bisping-vs-dan-henderson-fights-gets-a-title-shot.mma |url-status=dead }}{{cite web |url=http://www.sherdog.com/fighter/michael-bisping-10196 |title=Fight Finder – Michael \"The Count\" Bisping's Mixed Martial Arts Statistics |publisher=Sherdog.com |access-date=2009-05-15}}\n\nA preliminary match-up between [[Dong Hyun Kim]] and [[Jonathan Goulet]] was scheduled for the card, but Goulet withdrew due to a shoulder injury, and was replaced by [[T. J. Grant]].\n\n[[K-1 HERO'S]] Light Heavyweight Champion [[Yoshihiro Akiyama]] made his UFC debut against [[Alan Belcher]].\n\nThis event was [[Sherdog]]'s [[Sherdog#Event of the Year|2009 Event of the Year]].{{Cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/articles/1/Sherdogs-2009-Misc-Awards-22093|title = Sherdog's 2009 Misc. However, Franca suffered a torn ACL during training and could no longer participate in this event. He was replaced by [[Jeremy Stephens]].{{cite web |url=http://demandmma.com/news/hermes-franca-tears-acl-off-of-ufc-fight-night-17/ |title=Hermes Franca Tears ACL; Off of UFC Fight Night 17 |work=DemandMMA.com |date=January 26, 2009 |access-date=January 27, 2009 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090201135402/http://demandmma.com/news/hermes-franca-tears-acl-off-of-ufc-fight-night-17/ |archive-date=February 1, 2009 }}\n\nDerek Downey replaced [[Amir Sadollah]] in a bout against [[Nick Catone]]. Sadollah pulled out after being sidelined with a broken clavicle.\n\n[[Dan Miller (fighter)|Dan Miller]] replaced [[Alessio Sakara]] in a bout against [[Jake Rosholt]]. Sakara pulled out of the bout with a shoulder injury.\n\n[[Matt Veach]] replaced [[George Sotiropoulos]] against [[Matt Grice]].
