United Kingdom]]''.\n\nThis event was the UFC's first event in the state of [[Tennessee]] and it set the record in terms of attendance to date for both an [[Mixed martial arts|MMA]] event in the state and a UFC Fight Night show.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2009/04/ufcs-tennessee-debut-sets-ufc-fight-night-attendance-record |title=UFC's Tennessee debut sets UFC Fight Night attendance record |work=MMAjunkie.com |date=April 2, 2009 |access-date=December 3, 2014}}\n\nThe main event featured the debut of former [[List of WEC champions|WEC Welterweight Champion]] [[Carlos Condit]] – riding an eight fight win streak – in the main event against [[Martin Kampmann]]. Kampmann won the fight via split decision. However, the fight was close enough that FightMetric ruled the bout a draw.\n\nThe event drew a total of 1.9 million viewers.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2009/04/spike-tv-nabs-ratings-boost-with-ufc-fight-night-18-broadcast |title=Spike TV nabs ratings boost with UFC Fight Night 18 broadcast |work=MMAjunkie.com |date=April Diaz vs. Guillard]]\n}}\n'''''UFC 102: Couture vs. Nogueira''''' was a [[mixed martial arts]] [[pay-per-view]] event held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] (UFC) on August 29, 2009, at the [[Rose Garden (arena)|Rose Garden]] in [[Portland, Oregon]].{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=EventDetail.FightCard&eid=2225 |title=UFC 102 |publisher=Ufc.com |access-date=June 16, 2009 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090619051152/http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=EventDetail.FightCard&eid=2225 |archive-date=June 19, 2009 }} It featured former five-time [[List of UFC champions|UFC champion]] and [[UFC Hall of Fame]]r [[Randy Couture]], facing off against former [[List of Pride Fighting Championships champions#World Heavyweight Championship|PRIDE FC Heavyweight Champion]] and former [[UFC Heavyweight Championship|Interim UFC Heavyweight Champion]], [[Antônio Rodrigo Nogueira]].\n\n== Background ==\n\nRandy Couture was originally offered a title defense against Antônio Rodrigo Nogueira at [[UFC 81]], however turned the fight down after his resignation from the UFC in October 2007.{{cite web |url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=5189&zoneid=2 |title=Randy Couture turns down title defense |publisher=MMAWeekly.com |access-date=August 26, 2009 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20081013002534/http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=5189&zoneid=2 |archive-date=October 13, 2008 }} While Couture and the UFC sorted out their legal issues,{{cite web|url=http://www.lvrj.com/sports/13794082.html |title=Couture is target of UFC lawsuit - Sports - ReviewJournal.com |publisher=Review Journal.com |access-date=August 26, 2009}} Nogueira defeated [[Tim Sylvia]] for the [[UFC Heavyweight Championship|Interim UFC Heavyweight Championship]] becoming the first fighter to hold both [[Pride Fighting Championships|PRIDE]] and UFC title belts.{{cite web|author=Sherdog.com |url=https://www.sherdog.com/news/articles/1/nogueira-becomes-first-to-hold-ufc-pride-belts-11138 |title=Nogueira Becomes First to Hold UFC, PRIDE Belts |publisher=Sherdog.com |access-date=August 26, 2009}} Nogueira was then set to coach against [[Frank Mir]] on the [[The Ultimate Fighter: Team Nogueira vs Team Mir|eighth]] season of [[The Ultimate Fighter]] with the two facing off for the Interim Championship after the season aired.{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=news.detail&gid=11905 |title=UFC : Ultimate Fighting Championship |publisher=Ultimate Fighting Championship |date=May 12, 2008 |access-date=August 26, 2009 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20081029213233/http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=news.detail&gid=11905 |archive-date=October 29, 2008 }}\n\nEventually Couture and the UFC reconciled and it was announced that he would return to the Octagon to defend his title against [[Brock Lesnar]] at [[UFC 91]].{{cite web |last=Nate |first=Kid |url=http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2008/9/2/606204/ufc-91-randy-couture-vs-br |title=Major announcement: Randy Couture Returns to UFC; UFC 91: Randy Couture vs. Brock Lesnar in Las Vegas |date=2 September 2008 |publisher=Bloody Elbow |access-date=August 26, 2009 |archive-date=7 January 2009 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090107151532/http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2008/9/2/606204/ufc-91-randy-couture-vs-br |url-status=dead }} Neither Couture or Nogueira were successful in their defense efforts and then faced each other in what amounted to a \"losers bracket\" of the UFC's mini heavyweight tournament.{{cite web|date=November 16, 2008 |url=http://fiveouncesofpain.com/2008/11/16/ufc-91-results-and-recap/ |title=UFC 91: Results And Recap |publisher=Five Ounces of Pain |access-date=August 26, 2009}}{{cite Sports |publisher=Sports.yahoo.com |access-date=August 26, 2009}}\n\nA previously announced bout between [[Kyle Kingsbury]] and Razak Al-Hassan was moved to [[UFC 104]] in [[Los Angeles, California]] on October 24, 2009.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/14809/kyle-kingsbury-vs-razak-al-hassan-bout-moved-from-ufc-102-to-october.mma |title=Kyle Kingsbury vs. Razak Al-Hassan bout moved from UFC 102 to fall |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |access-date=May 8, 2009 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090510083529/http://mmajunkie.com/news/14809/kyle-kingsbury-vs-razak-al-hassan-bout-moved-from-ufc-102-to-october.mma |archive-date=May 10, 2009 }} [[Matt Hamill]] pulled out of a bout with [[Brandon Vera]] due to injury. [[Krzysztof Soszynski]] took his place.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/15570/injured-matt-hamill-replaced-by-krzysztof-soszynski-in-ufc-102-bout-with-brandon-vera.mma |title=Injured Matt Hamill replaced by Krzysztof Soszynski in UFC 102 bout with Brandon Vera |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |access-date=July 20, 2009 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090723050525/http://mmajunkie.com/news/15570/injured-matt-hamill-replaced-by-krzysztof-soszynski-in-ufc-102-bout-with-brandon-vera.mma |archive-date=July 23, 2009 }}\n\nA knee injury forced [[James Irvin (fighter)|James Irvin]] out of his bout with [[Wilson Gouveia]]. American English|date=March 2026}}\n{{Use mdy dates|date=September 2023}}\n{{Infobox television\n|image = TUF 10 promo.jpg\n|genre = [[reality television|Reality]], [[Broadcasting of sports events|Sports]]\n|runtime = 60 minutes\n|creator = [[Craig Piligian]], [[Frank Fertitta III]], [[Lorenzo Fertitta]], [[Dana White]]\n|starring = Dana White, [[Rampage Jackson|Quinton Jackson]], [[Rashad Evans]]\n|country = United States\n|network = [[Spike (TV channel)|Spike]]\n|first_aired = {{start date|2009|9|16}}\n|last_aired =\n}}\n\n'''''The Ultimate Fighter: Heavyweights'''''{{Cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=news.detail&gid=20065|title=Evans and Jackson to coach season 10 of \"The Ultimate Fighter\"|date=May 28, 2009|publisher=UFC.com|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090530083959/http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=news.detail&gid=20065|archive-date=May 30, 2009|access-date=May 28, 2009}} is the tenth installment of the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] (UFC)-produced [[reality television]] series ''[[The Ultimate Fighter]]''. In a press conference following ''[[UFC 98]]'', Dana White announced that filming for the season would begin on June 1, 2009. The season debuted on [[Spike TV]] on September 16, 2009, following [[UFC Fight Night 19]].{{Cite web|url=http://mmafrenzy.com/10506/rampage-and-evans-to-coach-on-tuf-10-jackson-machida-likely-for-september/|title=\"Rampage\" and Evans to Coach on TUF 10; Jackson-Machida Likely for September|last=Karkoski|first=Kris|date=May 28, 2009|publisher=MMAFrenzy.com|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20110810022155/http://mmafrenzy.com/10506/%E2%80%9Crampage%E2%80%9D-and-evans-not-machida-to-coach-on-tuf-10/|archive-date=August 10, 2011|access-date=May 28, 2009}}\n\nThe UFC and Spike TV held an open casting call at The Marriott Seattle Airport Hotel in [[Seattle, Washington]], on April 6, 2009. The casting called for mixed-martial artists in the [[Middleweight (MMA)|middleweight]], [[Light heavyweight (MMA)|light-heavyweight]] and [[Heavyweight (MMA)|heavyweight]] divisions. On the day of tryouts, 250 potential fighters appeared including UFC & [[International Fight League]] fighters and former [[National Football League]] football players. Notable applicants included NFL veterans [[Herbert Goodman]] & Rex Richards, UFC veteran Rob Yundt, and [[International Fight League|IFL]] veteran Aaron Stark.{{Cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/14488/the-ultimate-fighter-10-tryouts-net-250-applicants-three-nfl-vets.mma|title=\"The Ultimate Fighter 10\" tryouts net 250 applicants, three NFL vets|date=April 6, 2009|publisher=MMAJunkie.com|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20121013185316/http://mmajunkie.com/news/14488/the-ultimate-fighter-10-tryouts-net-250-applicants-three-nfl-vets.mma|archive-date=October 13, 2012|access-date=April 8, 2009}}{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2009/04/the-ultimate-fighter-10-tryouts-bring-jubilation-and-heartbreak/amp|title=\"The Ultimate Fighter 10\" tryouts bring jubilation and heartbreak|last=McCray|first=Brad|date=April 7, 2009|publisher=MMAJunkie.com|url-status=live|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20121013185347/http://mmajunkie.com/news/14502/the-ultimate-fighter-10-tryouts-bring-jubilation-and-heartbreak.mma|archive-date=October 13, 2012|access-date=April 8, 2009}} As with the previous season, all applicants at the tryouts were required to have a professional MMA record.{{Cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com//index.cfm?fa=news.detail&gid=18659 |title=Spike TV and UFC To Hold TUF 10 Open Casting Call on April 6th in Seattle |publisher=UFC.com |date=March 16, 2009 |access-date=April 2, 2009 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20010221231634/http://www.ufc.tv/Index1.htm |archive-date=February 21, 2001 }}\n\nThis season did not feature an elimination round as the past three seasons had. All competitors invited to [[Las Vegas Valley|Las Vegas]] for the show's taping participated in the entire season.{{Cite web|url=http://content.usatoday.com/communities/mma/post/2009/06/67532833/1|title=Kimbo to compete on TUF 10|last=Non|first=Sergio|date=June 2, 2009|publisher=[[USA Today]]|access-date=June 2, 2009}}\n\n==Cast==\n\n===Coaches===\n\n*;{{color box|silver|border=silver}} '''Team Rampage'''\n**[[Rampage Jackson|Quinton \"Rampage\" Jackson]], head coach\n**Tom Blackledge\n**[[Tiki Ghosn]]\n**[[Mike Dolce]], nutritionist\n*;{{color box|gold|border=silver}} '''Team Rashad'''\n**[[Rashad Evans]], head coach\n**[[Jackson's Submission Fighting|Greg Jackson]]\n**[[Trevor Wittman]]\n**[[Mike Van Arsdale]]\n**[[Rolles Gracie]]\n\n===Fighters===\n*'''Team Rashad'''\n**[[James McSweeney]], [[Brendan Schaub]], [[Justin Wren]], [[Jon Madsen]], [[Roy Nelson (fighter)|Roy Nelson]], Darrill \"The Boss\" Schoonover, [[Matt Mitrione]], [[Mike Wessel]]\n*'''Team Rampage'''\n**[[Kimbo Slice|Kevin \"Kimbo Slice\" Ferguson]], Abe Wagner, Demico Rogers, [[Wes Sims (fighter)|Wes Sims]], Scott Junk, [[Wes Shivers]], [[Marcus Jones (fighter)|Marcus Jones]], [[Zak Jensen]]\n\n===Others===\n*Host: [[Dana White]]\n*Narrator: [[Mike Rowe]]\n\n==Episodes==\n;'''Episode 1: Size Does Matter'''\n*Only 15 fighters are lined up until Dana White introduced internet street fighting sensation, Kimbo Slice.\n*The fighters divided up and trained separately with Rashad Evans and Quinton Jackson for evaluations.\n*White flipped a coin (silver for Rampage, gold for Rashad). The winner of the coin toss chose to either pick the first fighter or decide the first two match ups. The winning fighter's team had to win both fights to keep control of the fight match ups.\n*Evans won the coin toss and decided to pick the first fighter. This meant that Jackson got to pick the first fight.\n{| class=\"wikitable\"\n|-\n!Coach\n!1st Pick\n!2nd Pick\n!3rd Pick\n!4th Pick\n!5th Pick\n!6th Pick\n!7th Pick\n!8th Pick\n|-\n!Rashad\n|James McSweeney\n|Brendan Schaub\n|Justin Wren\n|Jon Madsen\n|Roy Nelson\n|Darrill Schoonover\n|Matt Mitrione\n|Mike Wessel\n|-\n!Rampage\n|Kimbo Slice\n|Abe Wagner\n|Demico Rogers\n|Wes Sims\n|Scott Junk\n|Wes Shivers\n|Marcus Jones\n|Zak Jensen\n|}\n*Jackson matches Wagner against Madsen.\n*'''Jon Madsen''' defeated Abe Wagner via unanimous decision after two rounds.\n*At the end of the episode, it is seen that the gash on Wagner's head is so deep, that the medic treating him can see his skull.\n\n;'''Episode 2: All About Survival'''\n*Roy Nelson had a discussion with Rashad Evans and the gold team's coaches about his attitude and whether he really wanted to train with the team.\n*Jackson matched James McSweeney against Wes Shivers.\n*Team Rashad went past their allotted training time, into the silver team's training time. Quinton Jackson approached Evans and jokingly asked the team to leave.\n*'''James McSweeney''' defeated Wes Shivers by majority decision after two rounds.\n*Evans matched Nelson against Kimbo Slice.\n\n;'''Episode 3: The Enemy'''\n*Keith Jardine appeared at Team Rashad's training session, in support of his friend's team.\n*Rashad Evans and Quinton Jackson had an argument over the scheduling of the match against each other. James McSweeney joined the argument and Evans pulled him away.\n*Marcus Jones again struggled in training, this time complaining of an apparent knee injury. Despite this, Jones expressed his desire to compete in the next fight.\n*'''Roy Nelson''' defeated Kimbo Slice by TKO (strikes) at 2:01 in the second round.\n\n;'''Episode 4: Snitch'''\n*Marcus Jones was shown sweating profusely, prompting concerns that he would not be able to fight.\n*Rashad Evans informed his team of the likely choices for the remaining fights.\n*Justin Wren disagreed with Evans' choice of fight in him against Scott Junk, owing to their friendship.\n*Matt Mitrione told Team Rampage the planned matches, which led to him becoming a pariah to the rest of his team, who subsequently labelled him as a snitch.\n*Evans matched Brendan Schaub against Demico Rogers.\n*'''Brendan Schaub''' defeated Demico Rogers by submission (anaconda choke) at 3:17 in the first round.\n*After the fight, Quinton Jackson did not meet with Rogers; instead, Evans gave him some words of encouragement.\n\n;'''Episode 5: Rude Awakening'''\n*Matt Mitrione injured his shoulder in training, but wished to fight soon, leading Team Rashad to question whether his shoulder was legitimately injured or merely sore.\n*Rashad Evans, in discussion with his assistant coaches, worried about putting Mitrione in the fight and continuing their undefeated streak.\n*Zak Jensen also became an outcast in the house after being accused of masturbating in the communal bathroom and passing out during practice.\n*Evans matched Justin Wren against Wes Sims.\n*Marcus Jones believed that Evans promised him the next fight and becomes very emotional when he is not picked.\n*'''Justin Wren''' defeated Wes Sims by submission (arm triangle choke) at 1:34 in the first round.\n*Following the match, Quinton Jackson walked away from the cage without checking on his fighter.\n*Later, in the locker room area, Evans approached Jackson about this, which led to them having another verbal confrontation.\n\n;'''Episode 6: 16 Sharks'''\n*[[Phil Nurse]], stand up coach for [[Georges St-Pierre]] and Evans, came to help his student at Team Rashad's training practice.\n*Rashad Evans matches Darrill Schoonover against Zak Jensen.\n*Quinton Jackson kept calling Schoonover \"Titties\" and during the fight announcement, Schoonover started swearing at Jackson, leading to a verbal confrontation between the two.\n*Team Rampage gave Jensen the nickname \"Linderman\" and Wes Sims started a poll of when Jensen would have a breakdown.\n*During a training session, Jensen suffered a large cut over his eye. It gets stitched up but there was some concern whether he can fight or wants to fight.\n*Tiki Ghosn hired an artist to draw a picture of Team Rashad. They hang it in the training center, but Evans took it down before anyone on his team could see. Team Rampage later presented it to the gold team gift wrapped.\n*'''Darrill Schoonover''' defeated Zak Jensen by submission (triangle choke) at 2:29 in the first round.\n\n;'''Episode 7: Voices In My Head'''\n*Rashad Evans announced the final two preliminary fights: Matt Mitrione vs. Scott Junk and Mike Wessel vs. Marcus Jones.\n*Evans and Quinton Jackson continued their war of words, with Evans claiming that Jackson quit on his fighters, not the other way around.\n*Wes Sims and James McSweeney created a bet as to when Mitrione will break down or snap, much like the previous weeks' bet about Zak Jensen.\n*During a training session, Jon Madsen and Mitrione nearly came to blows during sparring.\n*What happened was that Mitrone was told about Madsen saying that he was going to slap him behind his back. He then decided to go out 100%, more than what the coaches instructed.\n*Evans then approached Mitrione, questioning if he would have the same aggressive temper during the fight.\n*'''Matt Mitrione''' defeated Scott Junk by majority decision after two rounds.\n*Following the match, Jackson walked away from the cage, flung a water bottle, and destroyed a door leading to the locker room area.\n\n;'''Episode 8: One Soldier Left'''\n*Brendan Schaub drew an insulting caricature of Marcus Jones. Mike Wessel later adds \"sucks balls\" to the bottom of the picture, which angered Jones.\n*Darrill Schoonover again squared up to Quinton Jackson over his repeated \"titties\" jibes.\n*'''Marcus Jones''' defeated Mike Wessel by submission (armbar) at 0:50 in the first round.\n*Jones ends up being the first and only Team Rampage member to make it into the quarterfinals.\n*Dana White and the two coaches met with each quarter-finalist to find out who their preferred opponent in the next round was.\n*The quarterfinal fights were announced as:\n** Roy Nelson vs. Justin Wren\n** Brendan Schaub vs. Jon Madsen\n** James McSweeney vs. Matt Mitrione\n** Marcus Jones vs. Darrill Schoonover\n*Rashad Evans offered Team Rampage members the chance to train with him and later, he and Jackson clash once more.\n\n;'''Episode 9: Rattled'''\n*Matt Mitrione complains of severe headaches and [[nausea]] as a result of his match with Scott Junk. Some believed that he may have had a concussion and may not be able to continue in the tournament. He was sent to the hospital to get a medical evaluation.\n*As a practical joke, Quinton Jackson and his assistant coaches put chickens in the cars of the Team Rashad coaches.\n*Dana White visited the house and talked with Mitrione and has him claim that if the doctor clears him to fight, he will fight in his quarterfinal match.\n*'''Roy Nelson''' defeated Justin Wren by majority decision after two rounds.\n\n;'''Episode 10: Gut Check'''\n*Matt Mitrione said that the doctor who examined him said he had swelling of the brain as a result of his fight against Scott Junk. There is discussion among Team Rashad coaches as well as other fighters about whether Mitrione will be able to continue in the tournament.\n*Kimbo Slice had issues with his knee. He had an MRI done and an examination by a doctor. Slice was missing [[cartilage]] in his knee and faced the possibility of needing an operation.\n*The coaches challenge is 2 vs. 2 beach volleyball. Team Rashad defeated Team Rampage, earning Rashad Evans $10,000 and the gold team's fighters $1,500 each.\n*Evans talked with Mitrione and asked him if he wanted to fight. Mitrione was unable to give him a straight answer. Evans called White about the situation.\n*Dana White talked with all of the fighters in the training center. He told them that fighters get hurt and fighters fight hurt, but none of them are being forced to fight. He told Mitrione that they will not know if he is medically cleared to fight until the next day; if he is medically cleared, he needed to decide whether he wanted to fight.\n*'''Brendan Schaub''' defeated Jon Madsen by KO (punch) at 1:39 in the second round.\n\n;'''Episode 11: Road to the Finale'''\n''Note: This episode aired in the United States as a two-hour episode.''\n*Dana White wanted to talk with fighters for a possible replacement for Matt Mitrione.\n*White at first talked to Kimbo Slice who complained of knee problems and did not think he could fight to his fullest. Options were given to Slice, such as [[cortisone]] shots and other treatments, to improve the [[arthritis]] in his knee. However, he turned down the treatment and the possible fight.\n*Mitrione talked on the phone with his doctor and said he wanted to fight. The doctor cleared him to fight.\n*Scott Junk was having problems seeing following his fight. The doctor told him there were two small tears in his [[retina]] and that he must have surgery or he may go blind. There were questions about whether or not he will be able to fight again.\n*Marcus Jones took the news about Junk personally. He told Mitrione to give a reason to hit him and claimed that he killed Junk's career.\n*Junk got an update that the eye surgery was a success and he could start training again in two months time.\n*At the weigh-in, Mitrione and McSweeney got in each other's faces, and the Englishman shoved Mitrione away.\n*'''James McSweeney''' defeated Matt Mitrione by submission (guillotine choke) at 3:38 in the first round.\n*'''Marcus Jones''' defeated Darrill Schoonover by TKO (punches) at 2:37 in the first round.\n*The semifinal matches consisted of three, five-minute rounds. The semifinal matches were announced as:\n**Roy Nelson vs. James McSweeney\n**Marcus Jones vs. Brendan Schaub\n*McSweeney locked a claustrophobic Zak Jensen in the bathroom. After being let out, Jensen attacked McSweeney who put him in a standing guillotine choke until he calmed down.\n*'''Roy Nelson''' defeated James McSweeney by TKO (strikes) at 4:13 in the first round.\n*'''Brendan Schaub''' defeated Marcus Jones by TKO (punches) at 2:11 in the first round.\n\n==TV ratings==\n*\"TUF 10\" episode No. 1: 4.1 million viewers\n*\"TUF 10\" episode No. 2: 2.9 2009.{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=eventdetail.home&eid=2465 |title=TUF 10 Finale |publisher=Ufc.com |access-date=2009-10-13 |url-status=dead from ''The Ultimate Fighter: Heavyweights'' in the [[Heavyweight (MMA)|Heavyweight]] division. It also featured the official UFC debut of [[Kimbo Slice]].\n\nA previously announced bout between [[Rousimar Palhares]] and Lucio Linhares was moved to [[UFC 107]].{{cite web |url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=9667&zoneid=13 |title=PALHARES VS. [[Alessio Sakara]] who had to pull out due to rumored visa issues.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/16324/m-1-vet-lucio-linhares-headed-to-ufc-meets-rousimar-palhares-at-tuf-10-finale.mma fighters received $25,000 bonuses.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/17099/the-ultimate-fighter-10-finale-bonuses-nelson-bocek-edgar-veach-earn-25k-awards.mma|title=The Ultimate Fighter 10 2012}}\n\n*'''Fight of the Night: Frankie Edgar vs. Matt Veach'''\n*'''Knockout of the Night: Roy Nelson'''\n*'''Submission of the Night: Mark Bocek'''\n\n==Reported payout==\nThe following is the reported payout to the fighters as reported to the [[Nevada State Athletic Commission]]. It does not include sponsor money or \"locker room\" bonuses often given by the UFC and also do not include the UFC's traditional \"fight night\" bonuses.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2009/12/the-ultimate-fighter-10-finale-fighters-salaries-kimbo-slice-gets-25k|title=The Ultimate Fighter 10 Finale fighters salaries: Hamill, Edgar top earners, Kimbo gets $25K\n|publisher=mmajunkie.com |access-date=December 7, 2009}}\n\n*Roy Nelson: $16,000 ($8,000 win bonus) def. Brendan Schaub: $8,000\n*Matt Hamill: $46,000 ($23,000 win bonus) def. Jon Jones: $20,000\n*Kimbo Slice: $25,000 (no win bonus) def. Houston Alexander: $13,000\n*Frankie Edgar: $46,000 ($23,000 win bonus) def. Matt Veach: $5,000\n*Matt Mitrione: $16,000 ($8,000 win bonus) def. held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] (UFC) on September 19, 2009, in [[Dallas]], [[Texas]], at the [[American Airlines Center]].{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=EventDetail.FightCard&eid=2277 |title=UFC 103 |publisher=Ufc.com |access-date=July 14, 2009}} The event was broadcast on [[pay-per-view]].\n\n==Background==\nThis was the first UFC event to air a portion of the preliminary card live and commercial-free during an hour long block on [[Spike (TV channel)|Spike]].{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=news.detail&gid=22036 |title=SPIKE TV TO TELECAST LIVE UNDERCARD BOUTS FOR THE FIRST TIME |publisher=Ufc.com |access-date=August 21, 2009}} The [[Efraín Escudero]] vs. [[Cole Miller]], [[Drew McFedries]] vs. [[Tomasz Drwal]], [[Rick Story]] vs. [[Brian Foster (fighter)|Brian Foster]], and the [[Jim Miller (fighter)|Jim Miller]] vs. Steve Lopez fights were shown on Spike.\n\nAs a part of the UFC/ESPN UK regionalization strategy the bout of [[Paul Daley]] vs. Brian Foster was to air on [[ESPN (UK)]] prior to Daley replacing [[Mike Swick]] against [[Martin Kampmann]].{{cite web|last=Cofield |first=Steve |url=https://sports.yahoo.com/mma/blog/cagewriter/post/UFC-ESPN-regionalize-U-K-broadcasts-Daley-figh;_ylt=AqY.6yzing0.KZmFtdvT5V09Eo14?urn=mma,186150 |title=UFC/ESPN regionalize U.K. broadcasts: Daley fight a special feature at UFC 103. – Cagewriter – MMA – Yahoo! Sports |publisher=Sports.yahoo.com |date=August 31, 2009 |access-date=September 20, 2009}}\n\nThe event was aired the same time [[Floyd Mayweather Jr. vs. Juan Manuel Márquez|Floyd Mayweather fought Juan Manuel Márquez]]. Mayweather/Márquez drew a smaller crowd (13,116) but its gate was considerably higher at $6,811,300. It also sold more PPV buys (1,000,000).\n\nTo date, this PPV had the largest amount of fights broadcast by the ''UFC'' (13).\n\nIt was announced on July 20, 2009 that [[Rich Franklin]] would headline UFC 103 against [[Dan Henderson]].{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=news.detail&gid=21299 |title=Henderson-Franklin II to Headline UFC 103 |publisher=Ufc.com |date=July 31, 2009 |access-date=July 31, 2009 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090724081847/http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=news.detail&gid=21299 |archive-date=July 24, 2009 }} It was then announced on July 31, 2009 that fans were not happy with the announced headliner of Henderson-Franklin 2 so they changed the main event to feature Rich Franklin vs. [[Vitor Belfort]]. \"Fans didn't like it, so we changed it,\" UFC President Dana White said.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/15689/rich-franklin-vs-vitor-belfort-not-dan-henderson-announced-as-new-ufc-103-main-event.mma |title=Rich Franklin vs. Vitor Belfort, not Dan Henderson, new UFC 103 main event | MMAjunkie.com |access-date=2009-08-03 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090803081335/http://mmajunkie.com/news/15689/rich-franklin-vs-vitor-belfort-not-dan-henderson-announced-as-new-ufc-103-main-event.mma |archive-date=2009-08-03 }}\n\nA previously announced bout featuring [[Steve Steinbeiss]] was moved to [[UFC Fight Night: Diaz vs. Guillard]].{{cite web|url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=9122&zoneid=13 |title=STEINBEISS VS. JENSEN SET FOR UFC FIGHT NIGHT 19 |publisher=Mma Weekly |access-date=July 9, 2009}} A previously announced bout featuring [[Aaron Simpson (fighter)|Aaron Simpson]]{{cite web|url=http://mmafrenzy.com/10386/aaron-simpson-and-steve-steinbeiss-added-to-ufc-103/ |title=Aaron Simpson and Steve Steinbeiss Added to UFC 103 |publisher=MMAFrenzy.com |access-date=May 17, 2009}} was moved to [[UFC 102]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/15502/aaron-simpson-vs-ed-herman-slotted-for-ufc-fight-night-19.mma|title=Aaron Simpson vs. Ed Herman middleweight bout slotted for Fighting Championship]] (UFC) on November 14, 2009, in [[Manchester]], [[United Kingdom]] at the [[Manchester Evening News Arena]].{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=eventDetail.FightCard&eid=2367 |title=UFC 105 |publisher=Ufc.com |access-date=2009-09-09}} This event aired on the same day, via [[Broadcast delay|tape delay]], on [[Spike (TV channel)|Spike]] in the [[United States|U.S.]]{{cite web|url=http://mmamania.com/2009/07/16/zuffa-confirms-ufc-105-for-manchester-england-on-november-14-will-likely-air-free-on-spike-tv/ |title=Zuffa confirms UFC 105 for Manchester, England on November 14; will likely air free on Spike TV |date=16 July 2009 |publisher=Mmamania.com |access-date=2009-07-16}}\n\n==Background==\n\nA previously rumoured [[UFC Lightweight Championship|Lightweight Championship]] bout between [[B.J. Penn]] and [[Diego Sanchez]]{{cite web |url=http://insidefights.com/2009/08/21/source-penn-vs-sanchez-to-headline-ufc-105/ |title=Penn vs. Sanchez to headline UFC 105 |publisher=insidefights.com |access-date=2009-08-21 |archive-date=26 August 2009 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090826124715/http://insidefights.com/2009/08/21/source-penn-vs-sanchez-to-headline-ufc-105/ |url-status=dead }} did not take place at this event. At the [[UFC 102]] press conference, White said it was likely that the fight will be moved to a third event in November which was at the time still unannounced.{{cite web |last=Morgan |first=John |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/15992/penn-vs-sanchez-targeted-for-ufcs-third-show-in-november-white-hints-at-new-broadcast-deal.mma |title=Penn vs. Sanchez targeted for UFC's third show in November; White hints at new broadcast deal |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |access-date=2009-08-27 |archive-date=30 August 2009 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090830035301/http://mmajunkie.com/news/15992/penn-vs-sanchez-targeted-for-ufcs-third-show-in-november-white-hints-at-new-broadcast-deal.mma |url-status=dead }} It was later announced that the bout would take place at [[UFC 107]].{{cite web|url=http://mmafrenzy.com/11471/bj-penn-vs-diego-sanchez-now-slated-for-ufc-107-on-december-12-in-memphis/ |title=BJ Penn vs. Diego Sanchez Now Slated for UFC 107 on December 12 in Memphis |publisher=MMAFrenzy.com |date=2009-01-02 |access-date=2009-09-03}}\n\nA bout between [[Antônio Rogério Nogueira]] and [[Luiz Cane|Luiz Arthur Cane]] was moved to [[UFC 106]].{{cite web|author=Sherdog.com |url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/nogueira-cane-slated-for-ufc-105-19635 |title=Update: Nogueira-Cane Likely for UFC 106 |publisher=Sherdog.com |access-date=2009-09-12}} [[Dong Hyun Kim]] was forced to pull out of his bout with [[Dan Hardy]] due to an injury. He was replaced by [[Mike Swick]].{{citation needed|date=January 2020}}{{Cite web|url=https://www.cp24.com/swick-to-face-hardy-at-ufc-105-in-november-1.438432|title=Swick to face Hardy at UFC 105 in November|date=28 September 2009|access-date=6 March 2020|archive-date=25 March 2023|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230325073444/https://www.cp24.com/swick-to-face-hardy-at-ufc-105-in-november-1.438432|url-status=dead}}\n\nThe winner of Mike Swick vs. Dan Hardy was said to receive a title shot at [[Georges St-Pierre]] in early 2010.[http://mmajunkie.com/news/16323/mike-swick-vs-dan-hardy-likely-to-determine-no-1-contender-for-georges-st-pierre.mma Mike Swick vs. Dan Hardy to determine No. 1 contender for Georges St-Pierre] {{Webarchive|url=https://web.archive.org/web/20091001105351/http://mmajunkie.com/news/16323/mike-swick-vs-dan-hardy-likely-to-determine-no-1-contender-for-georges-st-pierre.mma |date=2009-10-01 }} This fight took place at [[UFC 111]]. A previously announced bout between [[Peter Sobotta]] and [[DaMarques Johnson]] was cancelled due to a military commitment for Sobotta.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/16336/ufc-105-fight-card-including-televised-main-card-officially-announced.mma |title=Full \"UFC 105: Couture vs. Vera\" card announced, Johnson vs. Sobotta scrapped web|url = http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2009/12/23/1218031/the-sky-is-not-falling-ufc-107|title = The Sky is Not Falling: UFC 107 Draws 620,000 Buys|date = 23 December 2009|access-date = 24 December dead}}\n|previousevent=[[The Ultimate Fighter: Heavyweights Finale]]\n|followingevent=[[UFC 108|UFC 108: Evans vs. Silva]]\n}}\n\n'''''UFC 107: Penn vs. Sanchez '''''was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] (UFC) on December 12, 2009, in [[Memphis, Tennessee]], at the [[FedExForum]].{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=EventDetail.FightCard&eid=2469 |title=UFC 107 |publisher=UFC.com |date=2009-10-13 |access-date=2009-10-13}}\n\n==Background==\nOn October 21, it was announced that a previous matchup between welterweight contenders [[Thiago Alves (fighter)|Thiago Alves]] and [[Paulo Thiago]] had been changed to Alves vs. [[Jon Fitch]] because Fitch's opponent for ''[[UFC 106]]'', [[Ricardo Almeida]], suffered an injury. Paulo Thiago fought on ''UFC 106'' on November 21 against UFC newcomer [[Jacob Volkmann]] and won the fight by unanimous decision.\n\nIt was then announced on October 30 that Fitch would again be without an opponent as Alves was forced to withdraw because of a [[posterior cruciate ligament]] tear.[http://mmajunkie.com/news/16672/report-thiago-alves-forced-to-withdraw-from-ufc-107-bout-with-jon-fitch.mma Thiago Alves forced to withdraw from UFC 107 bout with Jon Fitch (Updated)] It was later announced that Jon Fitch would face [[Mike Pierce]] at UFC 107.\n\n[[Paul Buentello]] was scheduled to face [[Todd Duffee]] on the card, but an injury forced Duffee to pull out.{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/16724/heavyweight-prospect-todd-duffee-forced-out-of-ufc-107-with-undisclosed-injury.mma.mma |title=Heavyweight prospect Todd Duffee forced out of UFC 107 with undisclosed injury |publisher=mmajunkie.com |access-date=2009-11-04 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20121013185541/http://mmajunkie.com/news/16724/heavyweight-prospect-todd-duffee-forced-out-of-ufc-107-with-undisclosed-injury.mma.mma |archive-date=October 13, 2012 }} Buentello faced [[Stefan Struve]] on the card.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/16810/stefan-struve-replaces-todd-duffee-meets-paul-buentello-on-ufc-107-main-card.mma |title=Stefan Struve replaces Todd Duffee, meets Paul Buentello on UFC 107 main card |publisher=MMAJunkie.com |date=2009-11-11 |access-date=2009-11-11 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://archive.today/20120710220924/mmajunkie.com/news/16810/stefan-struve-replaces-todd-duffee-meets-paul-buentello-on-ufc-107-main-card.mma |archive-date=2012-07-10 }}\n\nA middleweight bout between [[Alan Belcher]] and [[Wilson Gouveia]] was changed to a 195-pound catchweight bout after Gouveia asked for a 190-pound catchweight limit, but Belcher gave a counter-offer of 195 pounds.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=10223&zoneid=13 |title=BELCHER VS. GOUVEIA NOW CATCHWEIGHT BOUT (UPDATED)|publisher=MMAWeekly.com |date=2009-12-11 |access-date=2009-12-12}}\n\nAnother matchup scheduled for this card was a match-up between [[The Ultimate Fighter: Heavyweights]] coaches [[Rashad Evans]] and [[Rampage Jackson|Quinton Jackson]] but the bout was cancelled after Jackson was selected for the role of Cpl. [[B. A. Baracus]] in [[The A-Team (film)|The A-Team]] film. The fight would later happen at ''[[UFC 114]]''.\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main card}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Lightweight\n|[[B.J. UFC confirmed that [[Forrest Griffin]] would step in to face Ortiz.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/16429/forrest-griffin-replaces-mark-coleman-faces-tito-ortiz-at-ufc-106.mma|title=Forrest Griffin replaces Mark Coleman, faces Tito Ortiz at \"UFC 106: Lesnar vs. Carwin\"|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|access-date=2009-10-09|archive-date=2009-10-10|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20091010142535/http://mmajunkie.com/news/16429/forrest-griffin-replaces-mark-coleman-faces-tito-ortiz-at-ufc-106.mma|url-status=dead}}\n\nOn October 26, UFC Heavyweight Champion [[Brock Lesnar]] pulled out of his fight against [[Shane Carwin]] due to an illness that kept him from training.{{Cite web|url=https://sports.yahoo.com/mma/news;_ylt=Aj4NOMGUkLcmRjFi6DxnOk09Eo14?slug=ki-lesnar102609&prov=yhoo&type=lgns|title=Y! Griffin fight headline the UFC 106 card.{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=news.detail&gid=23331 |title=Lesnar-Carwin Bout Postponed due to illness |access-date=2015-07-12 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20091029104204/http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=news.detail&gid=23331 |archive-date=2009-10-29 }}\n\nPreviously announced match-ups of [[John }} and [[Kenny Florian]] vs. [[Clay Guida]]{{cite web |url=http://mma.fanhouse.com/2009/08/27/kenny-florian-clay-guida-likely-for-ufc-106-in-november |title=Kenny Florian-Clay Guida Likely for UFC 106 February 6, 2010, at the [[Mandalay Bay Events Center]] in [[Las Vegas, Nevada]].{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=eventDetail.Home&eid=2654|title=UFC 109: Relentless|publisher=UFC.com |date=December 8, 2009}}\n\n==Background==\nA previously rumoured bout between [[Renzo Gracie]] and [[Matt Hughes (fighter)|Matt Hughes]] did not take place on this card, but took place at [[UFC 112]].{{cite news|url=http://mmafrenzy.com/12182/renzo-gracie-vs-matt-hughes-off-for-ufc-109-still-likely-for-2010/|title= Renzo Gracie vs. Matt Hughes Off for UFC 109, Still Likely for 2010|publisher=mmafrenzy.com |access-date=November 15, 2009}}\n\nA Middleweight Championship bout between [[Anderson Silva]] and [[Vitor Belfort]] was confirmed by UFC President [[Dana White]],{{cite news |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/16947/silva-vs-akiyama-uncertain-for-ufc-110-st-pierre-vs-hardy-instead.mma |title=Silva vs. Akiyama uncertain for UFC 110; St-Pierre vs. Hardy targeted instead? |publisher=mmajunkie.com |access-date=November 21, 2009 |archive-date=May 25, 2012 |archive-url=https://archive.today/20120525144331/http://mmajunkie.com/news/16947/silva-vs-akiyama-uncertain-for-ufc-110-st-pierre-vs-hardy-instead.mma |url-status=dead }} although it was contingent on Silva's successful recovery from elbow surgery.{{cite news |url= http://www.fightersonlymagazine.co.uk/news/viewarticle.php?id=3446 |title= Anderson: \"Recovery not going as planned\" |publisher= Fighter's Only Magazine |access-date= November 18, 2009 |url-status= dead |archive-url= https://web.archive.org/web/20091123104244/http://www.fightersonlymagazine.co.uk/news/viewarticle.php?id=3446 |archive-date= November 23, 2009 |df= mdy-all }} Subsequently, the fight was rescheduled due to Silva's continued slow healing from surgery.{{Cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/17020/anderson-silvas-recovery-slow-not-fighting-vitor-belfort-at-ufc-109.mma |title=Anderson Silva's recovery slow, not fighting Vitor Belfort at UFC 109 |access-date=November 29, 2009 |archive-date=May 25, 2012 |archive-url=https://archive.today/20120525144333/http://mmajunkie.com/news/17020/anderson-silvas-recovery-slow-not-fighting-vitor-belfort-at-ufc-109.mma |url-status=dead }} The Silva vs. Belfort bout was expected to take place at [[UFC 112]], however Belfort was forced to withdraw from the fight and [[Demian Maia]] replaced him, after number one contender [[Chael Sonnen]] was not clear to fight.{{cite web|url=http://mmafrenzy.com/12675/anderson-silva-vs-vitor-belfort-in-the-works-for-ufc-112-in-abu-dhabi/|title=Anderson Silva vs. Vitor Belfort In The Works for UFC 112 in Abu Dhabi|publisher=mmafrenzy.com |date=January 6, 2010}}\n\n[[Antônio Rogério Nogueira]] had to withdraw from his bout against [[Brandon Vera]] due to an ankle injury.{{Cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/17230/little-nog-out-of-ufc-109-brandon-vera-vs-jon-jones-targeted-for-march-31.mma |title=\"Little Nog\" out of UFC 109; Brandon Vera vs. Jon Jones targeted for March 31 |access-date=February 10, 2010 |archive-date=May 25, 2012 |archive-url=https://archive.today/20120525144334/http://mmajunkie.com/news/17230/little-nog-out-of-ufc-109-brandon-vera-vs-jon-jones-targeted-for-march-31.mma |url-status=dead }} It was announced that Vera will face future [[UFC Light Heavyweight Championship|Light Heavyweight Champion]] [[Jon Jones]] on March 21, 2010, at [[UFC Live: Vera vs. Jones]].\n\n[[Josh Koscheck]] was scheduled to face [[Paulo Thiago]], but was forced off the card with an injury. [[Mike Swick]] stepped in as Koscheck's replacement and fought Thiago.{{cite web |url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2009/12/28/mike-swick-replaces-josh-koscheck-will-face-paulo-thiago-at-ufc/|title=Mike Swick Replaces Josh Koscheck, Will Face Paulo Thiago at UFC 109|date=December 28, 2009 |publisher=mmafighting.com|access-date=December 28, 2009}}\n\nThe bout between [[Randy Couture]] and [[Mark Coleman]] was originally scheduled for [[UFC 17]] in 1998, but a Couture injury forced the cancellation of the bout. Nearly twelve years later, this bout headlined UFC 109. It marked the first time in UFC history that two [[UFC Hall of Fame|Hall of Fame]] inductees fought in an event.{{cite web |url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/Couture-Coleman-Slated-for-UFC-109-21002/|title=Couture-Coleman Slated for UFC 109|publisher=sherdog.com|access-date=January 15, 2010}}\n\n[[Mostapha Al-turk]] ran into visa issues that prevented him from making the trip to Las Vegas his fight against [[Rolles Gracie]].{{cite web|url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=10613&zoneid=13 |title=AL-TURK IN DOUBT FOR UFC 109 WITH VISA ISSUES |publisher=mmaweekly.com |access-date=February 2, 2010 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20100131161642/http://www.mmaweekly.com/absolutenm/templates/dailynews.asp?articleid=10613&zoneid=13 |archive-date=January 31, 2010 }} Both [[Jon Madsen]]{{cite web|url=http://www.aroundtheoctagon.com/?p=3173|title=Mustapha Al-Turk may be off of UFC 109|publisher=aroundtheoctagon.com|access-date=January 29, 2010|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20100204020039/http://www.aroundtheoctagon.com/?p=3173|archive-date=February 4, 2010|df=mdy-all}} and [[Joey Beltran]]{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/17767/ufc-newcomer-joey-beltran-likely-to-replace-mustapha-al-turk-at-ufc-109.mma |title=UFC newcomer Joey Beltran likely to replace Mostapha Al Turk at UFC Gracie.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/17778/joey-beltran-official-as-late-replacement-faces-rolles-gracie-on-ufc-109-prelims.mma|title=Joey Beltran official as late replacement, faces Rolles 2010|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20100128001559/http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2010/1/25/1268218/dave-meltzer-ufc-108-estimated-at|url-status=dead}}\n|previousevent=[[UFC 107|UFC 107: Penn vs Sanchez]]\n|followingevent=[[UFC Fight Night: Maynard vs. Diaz]]\n}}\n\n'''''UFC 108: Evans vs. Silva''''' was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] on January 2, 2010, in [[Las Vegas, Nevada]], at the [[MGM Grand Garden Arena]].{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=eventDetail.Home&eid=2533|title=UFC 108 EVANS vs SILVA |publisher=Ultimate Fighting Championship|access-date=2009-11-18}}\n\n== Background ==\nThe card for UFC 108 was plagued with numerous injuries, with multiple potential fights being called off as a result.\n\nA proposed main event featured [[UFC Middleweight Championship|UFC Middleweight Champion]] [[Anderson Silva]] facing off against former [[UFC Light Heavyweight Champion]] [[Vitor Belfort]]. However, it was announced by Silva's manager, [[Ed Soares]], that the fight would not happen at UFC 108 because Silva would not be fully healed from elbow surgery.\n\nA bout between [[Brock Lesnar]] and [[Shane Carwin]] for the [[UFC Heavyweight Championship]] was rescheduled from ''[[UFC 106]]'' to UFC 108, but this fight did not take place due to a continuing medical condition suffered by Lesnar.{{cite web |url=http://www.mmanews.com/ufc/Brock-Lesnar-Not-Fighting-Shane-Carwin-At-UFC-108.html |title=Brock Lesnar Not Fighting Shane Carwin At UFC 108 |access-date=2009-11-05 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20091108100037if_/http://www.mmanews.com/ufc/Brock-Lesnar-Not-Fighting-Shane-Carwin-At-UFC-108.html |archive-date=2009-11-08}}\n\nThe winner of a bout between [[Antônio Rodrigo Nogueira]] and [[Cain Velasquez]] was to be guaranteed a title shot for the [[UFC Heavyweight Championship]] against the eventual winner of the Lesnar vs. Carwin bout.{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/16806/ufc-108s-nogueira-vs-velasquez-winner-guaranteed-title-shot.mma|title=UFC 108's Nogueira vs Velasquez winner guaranteed title shot|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=2009-11-10|access-date=2009-11-10|archive-date=2012-05-25|archive-url=https://archive.today/20120525145014/http://mmajunkie.com/news/16806/ufc-108s-nogueira-vs-velasquez-winner-guaranteed-title-shot.mma|url-status=dead}} The planned bout was later scrapped when Nogueira was forced to pull out of the fight due to a severe case of staph infection.{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/16855/nogueira-has-sever-staph-infection-ufc-108-bout-with-velasquez-off.mma|title=Nogueira has severe staph infection; UFC 108 bout with Velasquez off|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=2009-11-14|access-date=2009-11-14|archive-date=2009-11-17|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20091117151819/http://mmajunkie.com/news/16855/nogueira-has-sever-staph-infection-ufc-108-bout-with-velasquez-off.mma|url-status=dead}} A possible Velasquez vs. Carwin match for an interim belt had been ruled out as a new main event due to Carwin undergoing knee surgery,{{cite news|url=http://www.ifight365.com/2009/11/ufc-brock-lesnar-seriously-ill-out-indefinitely/|title=UFC – Brock Lesnar seriously ill, out indefinitely |publisher=ifight365.com|date=2009-11-15}} although there were talks of the fight possibly happening at a later date.{{cite web | url=http://fiveouncesofpain.com/2009/11/18/shane-carwin-in-talks-to-fight-cain-velasquez-for-interim-title/ | title=MMA Archives}}{{Cite web | url=http://www.fightersonlymagazine.co.uk/news/viewarticle.php?id=3444 | title=UFC to create interim title, Lesnar | date=2009-11-18 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20111004215700/http://www.fightersonlymagazine.co.uk/news/viewarticle.php?id=3444 | archive-date=2011-10-04}} The Nogueira/Velasquez bout was scheduled for February 21, 2010 at [[UFC 110]],{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/17194/nogueira-vs-velasquez-bisping-vs-silva-official-for-ufc-110-nogueira-vs-velasquez.mma|title=Nogueira vs. Velasquez, Bisping vs. Silva official for \"UFC 110: Nogueira vs. Velasquez\"|publisher=mmafrenzy.com|date=2009-12-13|archive-date=2009-12-15|access-date=2009-12-14|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20091215215357/http://mmajunkie.com/news/17194/nogueira-vs-velasquez-bisping-vs-silva-official-for-ufc-110-nogueira-vs-velasquez.mma|url-status=dead}} though it was not for the interim belt.\n\n[[Gabriel Gonzaga]] was forced to pull out of his bout with [[Junior dos Santos]] on November 25, due to illness.{{cite news|url=https://sports.yahoo.com/mma/blog/cagewriter/post/Another-good-fight-goes-bye-bye-Gonzaga-out-of-?urn=mma,204974|title=Another good fight goes bye-bye: Gonzaga out of UFC 108|publisher=sports.yahoo.com|date=2009-11-25}} Gonzaga was replaced by [[Gilbert Yvel]].{{cite news|url=http://fightclubhouse.com/news/gilbert-yvel-replaces-gabriel-gonzaga-at-ufc-108/|title=Gilbert Yvel Replaces Gabriel Gonzaga at UFC 108|publisher=fightclubhouse.com|date=2009-12-03}}\n\n[[Paul Daley]] was scheduled to face [[Carlos Condit]], but on December 9, it was announced that Condit had pulled out due to injury.{{cite news|url=http://www.mmaunltd.com/news-detail.asp/NewsID/1127/condit-out-of-ufc-108-bout-against-daley.htm|title=Condit pulls from UFC 108 bout against Daley|publisher=mmaunltd.com|date=2009-12-09}} Condit was replaced by [[Dustin Hazelett]].{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/17145/dustin-hazelett-verbally-agrees-to-replace-carlos-condit-face-paul-daley-at-ufc-108.mma |title=Dustin Hazelett verbally agrees to replace Carlos Condit, face Paul Daley at UFC 108 {{!}} MMAjunkie.com |website=mmajunkie.com |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20091213042435/http://mmajunkie.com/news/17145/dustin-hazelett-verbally-agrees-to-replace-carlos-condit-face-paul-daley-at-ufc-108.mma |archive-date=2009-12-13}}\n\nOn December 11, [[Tyson Griffin]] pulled out of his bout with [[Jim Miller (fighter)|Jim Miller]], citing an injury.{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/17172/ufc-108-evans-vs-silva-takes-another-hit-lightweight-tyson-griffin-out.mma|title= \"UFC 108: Evans vs. Silva\" takes another hit; lightweight Tyson Griffin out |publisher=mmajunkie.com |access-date=2009-12-11}} [[Sean Sherk]], who originally had a fight with [[Rafaello Oliveira]], stepped up as Griffin's replacement.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/17178/sean-sherk-promoted-to-ufc-108-main-card-faces-jim-miller.mma |title=Sean Sherk promoted to \"UFC 108: Evans vs. Silva\" main card, faces Jim Miller {{!}} MMAjunkie.com |website=mmajunkie.com |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20091214021705/http://mmajunkie.com/news/17178/sean-sherk-promoted-to-ufc-108-main-card-faces-jim-miller.mma |archive-date=2009-12-14}} However, on December 17, Sherk pulled out of the fight; [[Duane Ludwig]] would eventually replace Sherk.{{Cite web|url=http://nwitimes.com/sports/mixed-martial-arts/article_4fef556e-eb63-11de-b64b-001cc4c03286.html|title = Source: Sherk off UFC 108 card with another injury| date=17 December 2009}}\n\n[[Martin Kampmann]] vs. [[Rory Markham]] was also called off days later due to a knee injury. On December 14 it was announced that [[Jacob Volkmann]] would be replacing Markham. Volkmann came off short rest after last fighting at ''[[UFC 106]]''.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/17208/martin-kampmann-vs-replacement-jacob-volkmann-is-on-for-ufc-108.mma |title=Martin Kampmann vs. replacement Jacob Volkmann \"is on\" for UFC 108 {{!}} MMAjunkie.com |website=mmajunkie.com |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20091217104716/http://mmajunkie.com/news/17208/martin-kampmann-vs-replacement-jacob-volkmann-is-on-for-ufc-108.mma |archive-date=2009-12-17}}\n\nOn December 29, it was announced that [[Steve Cantwell]] had pulled out of his scheduled bout with [[Vladimir Matyushenko]]. Since there was no time to find a suitable replacement to face Matyushenko, the bout was called off.{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/17361/steve-cantwell-vs-vladimir-matyushenko-pulled-from-ufc-108-fight-card.mma|title=Steve Cantwell vs. Vladimir Matyushenko pulled from UFC 108 fight $2,500,000\n|buyrate= 240,000{{Cite web|url = http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2010/3/10/1367522/ufc-110-does-weak-ppv-buys|title = UFC 110 does weak PPV buys according to Dave Meltzer (via subscription https://web.archive.org/web/20100524061310/http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2010/3/10/1367522/ufc-110-does-weak-ppv-buys|url-status = dead}}\n|previousevent=[[UFC 109|UFC 109: Relentless]]\n|followingevent=[[UFC Live: Vera vs. Jones]]\n}}\n'''''UFC 110: Nogueira vs. Velasquez''''' was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] (UFC) on Sunday, February 21, 2010, in [[Sydney]], Australia, at [[Acer Arena]]. The event marked the UFC's first trip to Australia. The main card was scheduled for 2:00 pm Sunday in Sydney to cater to the U.S. audience, meaning that the event took place at the regular U.S. time (10:00 pm ET, on Saturday February 20).\n\nThe event drew 17,431 fans, a sell out of {{A$|2.5 million}}. 400 tickets were sold to a closed circuit viewing party at the Arena's Ballroom, making the number of total fans inside the building 17,831. The event grossed $540,000 in merchandise sales, breaking the previous record of $498,000 at [[UFC 83]]. Total sales topped the previous Acer Arena record held by [[Iron Maiden]].{{Cite web|url=https://www.bloodyelbow.com/2010/3/1/1331707/sportsbusiness-journal-ufc-sets|title=SportsBusiness Journal: UFC Sets Company Merchandise Sales Record in Australia|first=Michael|last=Rome|date=1 March 2010|website=Bloody Elbow|access-date=11 July 2022|archive-date=29 January 2023|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230129022028/https://www.bloodyelbow.com/2010/3/1/1331707/sportsbusiness-journal-ufc-sets|url-status=dead}}\n\n==Background==\nThe long expected bout between [[Yoshihiro Akiyama]] and [[Wanderlei Silva]]{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/16515/wanderlei-silva-confirms-rumored-ufc-110-headliner-with-yoshihiro-akiyama-in-australia.mma|title=Wanderlei Silva confirms rumored UFC 110 headliner with Yoshihiro Akiyama in Australia|publisher=mmajunkie.com|access-date=16 October 2009|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20091019040239/http://mmajunkie.com/news/16515/wanderlei-silva-confirms-rumored-ufc-110-headliner-with-yoshihiro-akiyama-in-australia.mma|archive-date=19 October 2009}} did not take place at this event. Instead, Silva faced [[Michael Bisping]].{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/17194/nogueira-vs-velasquez-bisping-vs-silva-official-for-ufc-110-nogueira-vs-velasquez.mma|title=Bisping vs. Silva official for \"UFC 110: Nogueira vs. Velasquez\"|publisher=mmajunkie.com|access-date=13 December 2009|archive-date=15 December 2009|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20091215215357/http://mmajunkie.com/news/17194/nogueira-vs-velasquez-bisping-vs-silva-official-for-ufc-110-nogueira-vs-velasquez.mma|url-status=dead}}\n\nA bout between [[Nate Marquardt]] and [[Chael Sonnen]] which was expected to take place on this card was moved to [[UFC 109]].{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/16938/nate-marquardt-vs-chael-sonnen-middleweight-bout-set-for-ufc-109-not-ufc-110.mma|title=Nate Marquardt vs. Chael Sonnen middleweight bout set for UFC 109 (not UFC 110)|publisher=mmajunkie.com|access-date=20 November 2009|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20091123072514/http://mmajunkie.com/news/16938/nate-marquardt-vs-chael-sonnen-middleweight-bout-set-for-ufc-109-not-ufc-110.mma|archive-date=23 November 2009}} A bout between [[Keith Jardine]] and [[Rich Franklin]] had been discussed,{{cite news|url=http://www.fiveknuckles.com/mma-news/Rich-Franklin-vs-Keith-Jardine-possible-for-UFC-110.html|title=Rich Franklin vs. Keith Jardine possible for UFC 110|publisher=fiveknuckles.com|access-date=12 November 2009|url-status=usurped|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20091115133656/http://www.fiveknuckles.com/mma-news/Rich-Franklin-vs-Keith-Jardine-possible-for-UFC-110.html|archive-date=15 November 2009}} but Jardine eventually fought the Ultimate Fighter 8 winner [[Ryan Bader]].\n\n[[Chris Lytle]] was expected to face [[Dong Hyun Kim]],{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/16946/dong-hyun-kim-vs-chris-lytle-set-for-ufc-110-in-australia.mma|title=Dong Hyun Kim vs. Chris Lytle set for UFC 110 in Australia|publisher=mmajunkie.com|access-date=21 November 2009|archive-date=23 November 2009|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20091123094358/http://mmajunkie.com/news/16946/dong-hyun-kim-vs-chris-lytle-set-for-ufc-110-in-australia.mma|url-status=dead}} but Kim was forced off the card with an injury.{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/17182/dong-hyun-kim-withdraws-from-ufc-110-chris-lytle-currently-without-opponent.mma|title=Dong Hyun Kim withdraws from UFC 110; Chris Lytle currently without opponent|publisher=mmajunkie.com|access-date=12 December 2009|archive-date=15 December 2009|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20091215031615/http://mmajunkie.com/news/17182/dong-hyun-kim-withdraws-from-ufc-110-chris-lytle-currently-without-opponent.mma|url-status=dead}} [[Brian Foster (fighter)|Brian Foster]] stepped in to fight Lytle.\n\nA bout between [[Elvis Sinosic]] and [[Chris Haseman]] was due to be the first ever all-Australian bout to take place in the UFC.{{Cite web|url=https://www.ufc.com/events?fa=news.detail&gid=33009|title=UFC Fight Cards, Watch Times, Live Stats | UFC.com|website=www.ufc.com}} However, a shoulder injury forced Sinosic out of the fight on 18 February, and bout was pulled from the card as a result.{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=news.detail&gid=78621 |title=Changes to UFC 110 Card |access-date=9 July 2010 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20100222195720/http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=news.detail&gid=78621 |archive-date=22 February 2010 }}\nAdditionally, [[Ben Rothwell]] was sidelined by an illness, leading to Australian veteran [[Anthony Perosh]] stepping in to face [[Mirko Cro Cop]].\n\nTickets for the event sold out on the first day they were available to the general public.{{cite web | url=http://premier.ticketek.com.au/shows/show.aspx?sh=ULTFIGHT10&searchId=316fad9e-0996-4478-b2d3-f27a14e471e4 | title=Ticketek Australia }} It was, at the time, the second fastest sellout of an event in the history of the UFC, behind [[UFC 83]] (which was the first ever UFC event in Canada). This record was later broken by [[UFC 127]], the second event to be held in Australia. UFC 110 aired live on Australian free-to-air sports channel [[10 Bold|ONE HD]].{{Cite web|url=https://www.ufc.com/events?fa=news.detail&gid=33993|title=UFC Fight Cards, Watch Times, Live Stats | UFC.com|website=www.ufc.com}}\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main card}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Heavyweight\n|[[Cain Velasquez]]\n|def.\n|[[Antônio Rodrigo Nogueira]]\n|KO (punches)\n|1\n|2:20\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Middleweight\n|[[Wanderlei Silva]]\n|def.\n|[[Michael Bisping]]\n|Decision (unanimous) (29–28, 29–28, 29–28)\n|3\n|5:00\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Lightweight\n|[[George Sotiropoulos]]\n|def.\n|[[Joe Stevenson]]\n|Decision (unanimous) (30–27, 30–27, 30–27)\n|3\n|5:00\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Light Heavyweight\n|[[Ryan Bader]]\n|def.\n|[[Keith Jardine]]\n|KO (flying knee and punch)\n|3\n|2:10\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Heavyweight\n|[[Mirko Cro Cop]]\n|def.\n|[[Anthony Perosh]]\n|TKO (doctor stoppage)\n|2\n|5:00\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent card|Preliminary card|header=no}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Light Heavyweight\n|[[Krzysztof Soszynski]]\n|def.\n|[[Stephan Bonnar]]\n|TKO (doctor stoppage)\n|3\n|1:04\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Welterweight\n|[[Chris Lytle]]\n|def.\n|[[Brian Foster (fighter)|Brian Foster]]\n|Submission (kneebar)\n|1\n|1:41\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Middleweight\n|[[C. [[Fairfax, Virginia]].{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/index.cfm?fa=eventDetail.Home&eid=2592|title=UFC Fight Night Maynard vs Diaz|publisher=UFC.com|date=November 16, 2009|df=mdy-all}}\n\n==Background==\nHeadlining this event was a rematch between [[Gray Maynard]] And [[The Ultimate Fighter 5]] Winner [[Nate Diaz]], The two faced each other on The Ultimate Fighter 5 Semi-Finals in an amateur bout, which Diaz won via submission (guillotine choke).\n\n[[Nik Lentz]] was expected to face [[Jeremy Stephens]] at the event. However, Stephens pulled out of the bout with an injury and was replaced by [[Thiago Tavares]].{{cite news|url=http://www.mmamania.com/2009/12/9/1193892/ufc-fight-night-20-thiago-tavares|title= UFC expected to take place on this card.{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/16521/josh-koscheck-vs-mike-pierce-in-the-works-for-ufc-fight-night-20-in-january.mma|title=Josh Koscheck vs. Mike Pierce 19, 2009|df=mdy-all}} However, Koscheck was shifted from this event to a bout with [[Anthony Johnson (fighter)|Anthony Johnson]] at ''[[UFC 106]]'', while Pierce would face [[Jon Fitch]] at ''[[UFC 107]]''.{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/16642/josh-koscheck-vs-anthony-johnson-slotted-for-ufc-106-co-main-event.mma|title=Josh Koscheck vs. Anthony Johnson slotted for UFC 106 co-main It began production in January 2009, and began airing on [[Spike (TV channel)|Spike]] on April 1, 2009.{{Cite web |url=http://www.mmafighting.com/news/2009/02/21/ifl-vet-mark-miller-picked-the-ultimate-fighter-9 |title=IFL vet Mark Miller picked for 'The Ultimate Fighter 9' |date=February 21, 2009 |access-date=February 21, 2009 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090225055730/http://www.mmafighting.com/news/2009/02/21/ifl-vet-mark-miller-picked-the-ultimate-fighter-9 |archive-date=February 25, 2009 }} The season featured [[lightweight (MMA)|Lightweight]] fighters (146–155 lb) and [[welterweight (MMA)|Welterweight]] fighters (156–170 lb). In order to be considered for the show, a fighter had to have competed in at least three professional bouts prior to the tryouts in October 2008.{{Cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/13064/the-ultimate-fighter-u-s-vs-u-k-adds-lightweights-no-amateurs-allowed.mma|title=\"The Ultimate Fighter: U.S. vs. U.K.\" adds lightweights, no amateurs allowed|first=Kevin|last=Malone|author2=Stupp, Dann|publisher=MMAJunkie.com|date=October 21, 2008|access-date=January 12, 2009|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090122045523/http://mmajunkie.com/news/13064/the-ultimate-fighter-u-s-vs-u-k-adds-lightweights-no-amateurs-allowed.mma|archive-date=January 22, 2009}}\n\nUFC Middleweight fighter and winner of [[The Ultimate Fighter 3]], [[Michael Bisping]] served as one of the two coaches. The other coach was [[Dan Henderson]]. Henderson earned his place as a coach at [[UFC 93]] by defeating [[Rich Franklin]].\n\nBisping and Henderson fought each other at [[UFC 100]] on July 11, 2009.{{Cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/14123/the-ultimate-fighter-9-finale-slated-for-june-20.mma |title=The Ultimate Fighter 9 Finale slated for June 20 |first=Dann |last=Stupp |date=February 27, 2009 |access-date=February 27, 2009 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090301012828/http://mmajunkie.com/news/14123/the-ultimate-fighter-9-finale-slated-for-june-20.mma |archive-date=March 1, 2009 }} Henderson defeated Bisping at the event via second-round knockout.\n\n==Cast==\n\n===Coaches===\n* '''Team United States'''\n** [[Dan Henderson]], head coach\n** [[Cyrille Diabaté]], Muay Thai instructor\n** Ricardo Feliciano, Jiu-Jitsu instructor\n** Gustavo Pugliese, Boxing instructor\n** [[Heath Sims]], Wrestling instructor\n* '''Team United Kingdom'''\n** [[Michael Bisping]], head coach\n** Dave Jackson, Thai Boxing instructor\n** Mario Neto, Brazilian jiu-jitsu instructor\n\n===Fighters===\n* '''Team United States'''\n** '''Lightweights''': Santino DeFranco, [[Jason Dent]], Cameron Dollar, Richie Whitson\n** '''Welterweights''': [[DaMarques Johnson]], Frank Lester, Mark Miller, Jason Pierce\n* '''Team United Kingdom'''\n** '''Lightweights''': Jeff Lawson, [[Ross Pearson]], Martin Stapleton, [[Andre Winner]]\n** '''Welterweights''': Dean Amasinger, David Faulkner, [[Nick Osipczak]], [[James Wilks]]\n* '''US Fighters Eliminated in or before Entry Round:'''\n** '''Lightweights''': Paul Bird, John David-Shackelford (failed medical exam), Tommy Hayden, [[Waylon Lowe]], Rob Browning\n** '''Welterweights''': Steve Berger, Ray Elbe, Christian Fulgium (failed to make weight), [[Kevin Knabjian]], Kiel Reid\n* '''UK Fighters Eliminated in Entry Round:'''\n** '''Lightweights''': James Bryan, Dan James, Gary Kelly, A.J. Wenn\n** '''Welterweights''': James Bateman, Tommy Maguire, [[Che Mills]], [[Alex Reid (fighter)|Alex Reid]]\n\n===Others===\n*Host: [[Dana White]]\n*Narrator: [[Mike Rowe]]\n\n==Episodes==\n;'''Episode 1 – Bangers & Mashers'''\n*The episode was filmed at the Wolfslair MMA Academy in England.\n*Dana White announced that of the 16 competitors there, only eight would be flying to the United States. They fought in elimination matches to decide who made the trip.\n*The elimination matches were two rounds, with a third sudden victory round if there was a draw after the second round.\n*The fights actually occurred before UFC 93, so both Rich Franklin and Dan Henderson were present to observe the fights, as was Michael Bisping.\n*'''Andre Winner''' defeated Gary Kelly via KO (knees and punches) at 1:48 of round 1.\n*'''Jeff Lawson''' defeated James Bryan via submission ([[armbar]]) at 1:45 of round 1.\n*'''James Wilks''' defeated Che Mills via submission ([[Leglock#Heel hook|heel hook]]) at 0:30 of round 1.\n*'''Martin Stapleton''' defeated Dan James via submission (rear naked choke) at 1:28 of round 1.\n*'''Ross Pearson''' defeated A.J. Wenn via TKO (strikes) at 0:21 of round 2.\n*'''Nick Osipczak''' defeated Tommy Maguire via KO at 4:08 of round 1.\n*'''Dean Amasinger''' defeated Alex Reid via decision after three rounds.\n*'''David Faulkner''' defeated James Bateman via submission (heel hook) at 1:47 of round 1.\n\n;'''Episode 2 – Scars & Stripes'''\n* Dana White met with Dan Henderson at the UFC training center in Las Vegas, Nevada.\n* White met with the fighters and gave them a welcoming speech. Jason Pierce passed out due to difficulties in making weight.\n* John David-Shackelford was examined by a doctor and was discovered to have a contagious herpes lesion. He couldn't fight with it and was excused from the show.\n* Christian Fulgium failed to make weight at his first attempt at weighing in. He was given more time to try to make weight, but was unable to. White called him out and made him admit he quit the show.\n* Michael Bisping was not present during the U.S. elimination matches.\n* '''Mark Miller''' defeated Kevin Knabjian, his teammate from home, via TKO (punches) at 1:59 of round 2.\n* '''Richie Whitson''' defeated Paul Bird via submission (rear naked choke) at 3:11 of round 1.\n* '''Santino DeFranco''' defeated Waylon Lowe via submission (rear naked choke) at 0:45 of round 2.\n* '''Jason Pierce''' defeated Steve Berger via unanimous decision after three rounds.\n* '''Cameron Dollar''' defeated Tommy Hayden via technical submission (rear naked choke) in round 2.\n* '''DaMarques Johnson''' defeated Ray Elbe via TKO (strikes) at 3:10 of round 1.\n* Due to early departures by some fighters, only six fighters won an elimination round match to be part of Team USA. Kiel Reid and Jason Dent were the only two fighters who had not fought an elimination round match. White said they couldn't get into the house without fighting an elimination match, so two additional fighters were brought in.\n* The last two elimination round matches were announced for the next episode:\n** Jason Dent vs. Rob Browning\n** Kiel Reid vs. Frank Lester\n\n;'''Episode 3 – Red, White & Bruised'''\n* Jason Dent, Robert Browning, Kiel Reid and Frank Lester moved into The Ultimate Fighter house with the rest of the fighters, even though they had not fought their elimination matches.\n* Robert Browning, on the first night the fighters are in the house, started causing problems. He began by throwing eggs at a group of fighters on the basketball court, urinating in other people's shower and trying to get people to fight him.\n* '''Frank Lester''' defeated Kiel Reid via TKO at 3:16 of round 1. Referee Steve Mazzagatti stopped the fight while Lester had a kimura applied to Reid's arm. Reid appeared to be unconscious due to a slam he applied to Lester which resulted in Reid hitting his own head into the mat in the process.\n* '''Jason Dent''' defeated Robert Browning via TKO (strikes) at 3:36 of the first round.\n* Dana White flipped a coin to determine which team would pick the first match up. The match up decision will then alternate from one team to the other until all of the first round matches are complete. White flips the coin and Team USA got the first pick.\n\n;'''Episode 4 – Game On'''\n* Michael Bisping introduced his assistant coaches, Mario \"Sukata\" Neto and Dave Jackson.\n* Dan Henderson introduced his assistant coaches, Gustavo Pugliese, Ricardo Feliciano, Cyrille Diabate and Heath Sims.\n* Dan Henderson announced the first match up: Mark Miller vs. Nick Osipczak.\n* Due to visa issues, Bisping was unable to watch the U.S. elimination matches. He reviewed Mark Miller's match on videotape, then focused on training Osipczak.\n* Dana White gave his prediction that Miller would win the match.\n* '''Nick Osipczak''' defeated Mark Miller via KO (head kick) at 3:54 of round 2.\n\n;'''Episode 5 – Wiggity Wack'''\n* Michael Bisping announced the next match up: Andre Winner vs. Santino DeFranco.\n* DeFranco mentioned that he tried out for season two of ''The Ultimate Fighter''. After the medical exams, Dana White called him to tell him that he had two [[Cerebral aneurysm|brain aneurysms]] and needed to seek medical treatment. DeFranco was originally told he would not be able to fight again due to the medical condition; however, his doctor finally cleared him to fight.\n* '''Andre Winner''' defeated Santino DeFranco via TKO (strikes) at 3:49 of round 1.\n* A segment of the episode focused on the infighting occurring within Team USA. This was presented in contrast with the camaraderie of Team UK.\n* Dan Henderson announced the next match up: DaMarques Johnson vs. Dean Amasinger.\n* On the day of the fight, Michael Bisping was not present. Dana White says this was the first time in TUF history that a coach did not show up for a fight.\n* '''DaMarques Johnson''' defeated Dean Amasinger via submission (triangle choke) at 1:40 of round 1.\n\n;'''Episode 6 – $100 a Tooth'''\n* Michael Bisping announced the next fight: Martin Stapleton vs. Cameron Dollar.\n* Bisping apologised to Dean Amasinger for not showing up to Amasinger's fight. Bisping said he was asleep due to jet lag.\n* '''Cameron Dollar''' defeated Martin Stapleton via submission (rear naked choke) at 1:34 of round 1.\n* Dan Henderson announced the next fight: Frank Lester vs. James Wilks.\n* DaMarques Johnson challenged Lester to knock one of Wilks' teeth out during the match for $100.\n* After the first round of Lester's match, his four front top teeth were knocked out and stuck in his mouth guard, causing a delay starting the second round while they were removed.\n* '''James Wilks''' defeated Frank Lester via submission (armbar) at 3:06 of round 2.\n* In post-fight interviews, Lester revealed that, as a kid, his four front, top teeth were knocked out and replaced with false teeth. After the match, in the dressing room area, it was shown that Lester coughed up another tooth.\n\n;'''Episode 7 – The Bash Room'''\n* Richie Whitson had contracted a contagious bacterial infection, [[impetigo]], on the right side of his mouth. Jason Pierce sprayed disinfectant around the house where Richie had touched things.\n* There were conversations with Team USA coaches and teammates about Jason Pierce's work ethic and attitude. Jason Pierce said that the U.S. training was really frustrating for him.\n* Michael Bisping brought in a sledge hammer and a tractor tyre to train with. The fighters would swing the hammer and hit the tyre. Andre Winner missed the tyre twice, once breaking the mats the tire was sitting on. David Faulkner hit himself in the legs which causes an infection inside his leg.\n* In the coaches' challenge, Henderson and Bisping played tennis for $10,000. The fighters for the winning coach would win $1,500. '''Henderson''' defeated Bisping 6 games to 2.\n* Michael Bisping announced the next fight: Ross Pearson vs. Richie Whitson.\n* '''Ross Pearson''' defeated Richie Whitson via submission (armbar) at 3:40 of round 1.\n\n;'''Episode 8 – Negative Energy'''\n* Jason Pierce reported that he had a [[Staphylococcus|staph]] infection in his already injured foot and ankle. He wasn't sure if he could or would fight in his first round match.\n* Dave Faulkner had a troublesome gag reflex that caused him to spit out his mouthpiece. Michael Bisping worked with Faulkner in the ring to attempt to train him to keep the mouthpiece in his mouth.\n* Dan Henderson announced the next match: Jason Dent vs. Jeff Lawson.\n* Dave Faulkner, Jeff Lawson and Dean Amasinger held a falls count anywhere wrestling match in the house.\n* Dana White spoke with Jason Pierce and Dan Henderson. Pierce said that he was ready and able to fight, but acted like he didn't want to. White said he wasn't convinced that Pierce wanted to fight.\n* '''Jason Dent''' defeated Jeff Lawson via submission (anaconda choke) at 2:22 of round 2.\n* White spoke with Jason Pierce again. He informed Pierce that he wouldn't be allowed to fight because of the fighter's demeanour and attitude.\n\n;'''Episode 9 – Battle Royale'''\n* Dan Henderson informed Team U.S. that Jason Pierce would not be fighting in the next match. Dana White called in Frank Lester and Mark Miller and asked them if they would be willing to replace Pierce. Both were willing to do so.\n* Mark Miller was not medically cleared to fight due to a broken nose. Dana White decided that Frank Lester would return to the competition and fight David Faulkner even though he was missing four teeth.\n* Henderson asked Michael Bisping about the teams swapping training times. Henderson said that teams are allowed to switch training times each week. An argument occurred between Bisping and Henderson, as Bisping did not want to change to the early training time, but offered to have the Faulkner-Lester fight decide the issue. Henderson declined this challenge.\n* When the teams line up for the fight announcement, Bisping squirts water in the face of Damarques Johnson, as he wrongly believed that Johnson had made a racist comment during the coach challenge. They argued and Johnson left angrily. Bisping realised he had made a mistake (as the comment came from Dollar) and attempted to apologise.\n* David Faulkner was still having gag reflex problems related to his mouthpiece, so he went to see a [[hypnotherapist]] to help with the situation.\n* '''Frank Lester''' defeated Dave Faulkner after Dana White announces that the match will go to a sudden victory round. However, Dave Faulkner was exhausted and refused to fight another round.\n\n;'''Episode 10 – Smiling and Punching'''\n* Dana White and the coaches talked to the semi-finalists to see who they wanted to fight.\n* White announced the semi-final matchups:\n** Nick Osipczak vs. DeMarques Johnson\n** Andre Winner vs. Cameron Dollar\n** Ross Pearson vs. Jason Dent\n** James Wilks vs. Frank Lester\n* Dana White pointed out that Frank Lester was, at that point, the only fighter in TUF history to lose his first match in the house, come back as a substitute and win.\n* Richie Whitson overheard Jason Pierce talking with David Faulkner about how DeMarques Johnson trained. At a later training session, Dan Henderson approached Pierce and asked if Pierce was talking to Team UK about Team US' training sessions. Pierce denied that he was talking to Team UK.\n* '''DaMarques Johnson''' defeated Nick Osipczak via decision after three rounds.\n\n;'''Episode 11 – More Ups, Less Downs'''\n* Cameron Dollar had sore ribs, causing training to be difficult and painful.\n* '''Andre Winner''' defeated Cameron Dollar via submission (triangle choke) in round 1.\n* During training, Ross Pearson's shoulder popped out of the socket. He had Michael Bisping yank on his arm to put it back in place.\n* '''Ross Pearson''' defeated Jason Dent by decision after three rounds.\n* Dana White presented the lightweight, all UK, finalists: Andre Winner vs. Ross Pearson.\n\n;'''Episode 12 – All American Nightmare'''\n* Dan Henderson visited the TUF house and had a BBQ meal with Team U.S.\n* Frank Lester discussed with Dan Henderson, and later, James Wilks his attitude and demeanour towards their fight. He felt he was much calmer than in his first round match against Wilks.\n* Michael Bisping visited the TUF house and had dinner with Team UK. Dean Amasinger cooked dinner for the team. After Bisping left, Andre Winner played around spitting and throwing food at various Team UK members. Winner hit Amasinger with a mouthful of food. Amasinger chased Winner outside and threw Winner into the swimming pool. As Winner started climbing out of the pool, Dave Faulkner ran up and threw a bag of flour onto Winner. Everyone laughed it off as a good time.\n* '''James Wilks''' defeated Frank Lester via TKO (knees) in round 3.\n* The welterweight final is set: James Wilks vs. DaMarques Johnson.\n* [[Dana White]] said that even though Frank Lester lost to [[James Wilks]] twice, he was to him \"One of the toughest son of a bitches I've ever met.\"\n\n==Lightweight Bracket==\n{{Round16\n\n\n||'''Santino DeFranco'''|'''SUB'''|Waylon Lowe|2\n||'''Andre Winner'''|'''KO'''|Gary Kelly|1\n||'''Cameron Dollar'''|'''SUB'''|Tommy Hayden|2\n||'''Martin Stapleton'''|'''SUB'''|Dan James|1\n||'''Richie Whitson'''|'''SUB'''|Paul Bird|1\n||'''Ross Pearson'''|'''TKO'''|A.J. Fighter 9 Finale==\n\n{{Infobox MMA event\n|name=The Ultimate Fighter: United States vs. United Kingdom Finale\n|image=UFCSanchezGuida.jpg\n|promotion=[[Ultimate Fighting Championship]]\n|date=June
